#!/usr/bin/env node
// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000#8 — resolve once, pin core and adapters to that same release.
import { readFileSync, existsSync, writeFileSync, mkdirSync, readdirSync } from 'node:fs';
import { dirname, join } from 'node:path';
import { createRequire } from 'node:module';
import { spawnSync } from 'node:child_process';
import { previousVersion, installedVersion, acknowledgeVersion, compareVersions, installedHistory, runtimeChanges } from './release-changes.js';

function run() {
  const args = process.argv.slice(2).flatMap(arg => arg.startsWith('--to=') ? ['--to', arg.slice(5)] : [arg]);
  if (args.length === 1 && args[0] === '--help') { console.log('usage: snapback4 upgrade [--to <version>] [--json]\nUpgrade snapback4 and every installed snapback4-expo adapter with npm, bun or pnpm; keep exact pins. Default: latest; refuses downgrades unless --to is explicit. Rebuild native apps after changing the adapter.'); return; }
  const json = args.includes('--json');
  const input = args.filter(arg => arg !== '--json');
  const explicit = input[0] === '--to';
  const target = explicit ? input[1] : input[0];
  if (input.length !== (explicit ? 2 : target ? 1 : 0) || (explicit && target === 'latest') || (target && !/^(latest|\d+\.\d+\.\d+(?:-[0-9A-Za-z.-]+)?)$/.test(target))) throw Error('E_INPUT: upgrade takes [--to <exact version>] [--json]');
  const log = text => (json ? process.stderr : process.stdout).write(text + '\n');
  let root = process.cwd();
  while (!existsSync(join(root, 'package.json'))) { const parent = dirname(root); if (parent === root) throw Error('E_UPGRADE: no project package.json'); root = parent; }
  const pkg = JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(root, 'package.json'), 'utf8'));
  const previous = previousVersion(root);
  const installed = installedVersion(root);
  const schema = existsSync(join(root, 'snapback')) && readdirSync(join(root, 'snapback')).some(file => file.endsWith('.q'));
  // Resolve in a fresh Node process: pnpm can replace the package symlink,
  // while this helper's module/realpath cache still points at the old release.
  const checkScript = `
import { createRequire } from 'node:module';
import { dirname, join } from 'node:path';
import { spawnSync } from 'node:child_process';
try {
  const root = process.argv[1], require = createRequire(join(root, 'package.json'));
  const cli = join(dirname(dirname(require.resolve('snapback4/bin-path'))), 'bin/snapback4.js');
  const result = spawnSync(process.execPath, [cli, 'check', ...process.argv.slice(2)], { cwd: root, stdio: 'inherit' });
  process.exit(result.status ?? 1);
} catch { console.error('E_UPGRADE: cannot load installed snapback4 for check'); process.exit(1); }
`;
  const check = (baseline = false) => spawnSync(process.execPath, ['--input-type=module', '-e', checkScript, root, ...(baseline || json ? ['--json'] : [])], { cwd: root, env: { ...process.env, ...(baseline ? { SNAPBACK4_UPGRADE_BASELINE: '1' } : {}) }, encoding: 'utf8', stdio: ['ignore', 'pipe', 'pipe'] });
  const declared = pkg.packageManager?.split('@')[0];
  const locks = [['bun', ['bun.lock', 'bun.lockb']], ['pnpm', ['pnpm-lock.yaml']], ['npm', ['package-lock.json', 'npm-shrinkwrap.json']]].filter(([, files]) => files.some(file => existsSync(join(root, file)))).map(([pm]) => pm);
  if (declared && !['npm', 'bun', 'pnpm'].includes(declared)) throw Error('E_UPGRADE: supported package managers are npm, bun and pnpm');
  if (!declared && locks.length > 1) throw Error('E_UPGRADE: multiple package-manager lockfiles; set packageManager in package.json');
  const pm = declared ?? locks[0] ?? 'npm';
  const dependencies = ['dependencies', 'devDependencies', 'optionalDependencies'];
  const targets = ['snapback4', 'snapback4-expo'].filter(name => name === 'snapback4' || dependencies.some(key => pkg[key]?.[name]) || existsSync(join(root, 'node_modules', name, 'package.json')));
  const invoke = (command, capture = false) => {
    const result = spawnSync(pm, command, { cwd: root, encoding: 'utf8', stdio: capture ? ['ignore', 'pipe', 'pipe'] : json ? ['ignore', 2, 2] : 'inherit' });
    if (result.error || result.status !== 0) throw Error(`E_UPGRADE: ${pm} ${command[0]} failed; check registry/tool availability`);
    return result.stdout;
  };
  let version = target ?? 'latest';
  if (version === 'latest') {
    const text = invoke(pm === 'bun' ? ['info', 'snapback4@latest', 'version', '--json'] : ['view', 'snapback4@latest', 'version', '--json'], true).trim();
    try { const data = JSON.parse(text); version = typeof data === 'string' ? data : data.version; }
    catch { throw Error('E_UPGRADE: package manager returned no exact core version'); }
  }
  if (typeof version !== 'string' || !/^\d+\.\d+\.\d+(?:-[0-9A-Za-z.-]+)?$/.test(version)) throw Error('E_UPGRADE: registry returned no exact core version');
  if (!explicit && installed && compareVersions(version, installed) < 0) throw Error(`E_UPGRADE: requested ${target ?? "registry latest"} resolves to ${version} is older than installed ${installed}; refusing a downgrade. Use --to ${version} to request it explicitly.`);
  // Check the adapter release before any install changes package.json.
  for (const name of targets) invoke(pm === 'bun' ? ['info', `${name}@${version}`, 'version', '--json'] : ['view', `${name}@${version}`, 'version', '--json'], true);
  if (previous) acknowledgeVersion(root, previous);
  if (schema && installed === previous) {
    // A previous package may predate snapshots. Capture its own compiler output
    // before install; never infer old facts using the newly installed engine.
    const old = check(true);
    let facts;
    try { facts = JSON.parse(old.stdout); } catch { /* Unavailable baseline remains explicit. */ }
    const api = join(root, 'snapback/generated/api.ts');
    if (facts && existsSync(api) && old.status === 0) {
      const counts = values => (values ?? []).reduce((out, value) => { out[value.code] = (out[value.code] ?? 0) + 1; return out; }, {});
      const require = createRequire(join(root, 'package.json'));
      const version = JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(dirname(dirname(require.resolve('snapback4/bin-path'))), 'package.json'), 'utf8')).version;
      const sources = readdirSync(join(root, 'snapback')).filter(file => file.endsWith('.q')).sort((a, b) => a === 'schema.q' ? -1 : b === 'schema.q' ? 1 : a.localeCompare(b)).map(file => [file, readFileSync(join(root, 'snapback', file), 'utf8')]);
      mkdirSync(join(root, '.snapback4'), { recursive: true });
      writeFileSync(join(root, '.snapback4/check-surface.json'), JSON.stringify({ version, sources, api: readFileSync(api, 'utf8'), notes: counts(facts.notes), refusals: counts(facts.problems) }));
    } else {
      if (previous) {
        mkdirSync(join(root, '.snapback4'), { recursive: true });
        writeFileSync(join(root, '.snapback4/upgrade-version.json'), JSON.stringify({ version: previous }));
      }
      log(`upgrade: old check did not complete; ${previous ? 'runtime history is still available from the previous package version' : 'previous package version is unavailable; see guide changes'}`);
    }
  }
  // Preserve each dependency section. One resolved version, even across sections.
  const groups = new Map();
  for (const name of targets) {
    const sections = dependencies.filter(key => pkg[key]?.[name]);
    if (sections.length > 1) throw Error(`E_UPGRADE: ${name} occurs in multiple dependency sections; consolidate it first`);
    const section = sections[0] ?? 'dependencies';
    if (!groups.has(section)) groups.set(section, []);
    groups.get(section).push(`${name}@${version}`);
  }
  for (const [section, packages] of groups) {
    const mode = section === 'devDependencies' ? (pm === 'bun' ? '--dev' : '--save-dev') : section === 'optionalDependencies' ? (pm === 'bun' ? '--optional' : '--save-optional') : null;
    invoke([pm === 'npm' ? 'install' : 'add', pm === 'bun' ? '--exact' : '--save-exact', ...(mode ? [mode] : []), ...packages]);
  }
  const after = JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(root, 'package.json'), 'utf8'));
  for (const name of targets) if (!dependencies.some(key => after[key]?.[name] === version)) throw Error(`E_UPGRADE: ${name} was not pinned to ${version}`);
  const exact = existsSync(join(root, 'exact.mjs')) || existsSync(join(root, 'exact.json')) || dependencies.some(key => Object.keys(pkg[key] ?? {}).some(name => /^exact2?(?:$|[-/])/.test(name)));
  if (exact) log('Exact device rebuild: bun exact.mjs snapback --rebuild'); // HS owns this command and the pairing gate.
  let history = '', checkedFacts = null;
  if (!schema) {
    try {
      history = previous ? runtimeChanges(installedHistory(root), previous, version) : '';
      if (history) (json ? process.stderr : process.stdout).write(history);
      acknowledgeVersion(root, version);
    } catch { log('runtime history unavailable in installed package; run snapback4 guide changes'); }
  }
  if (schema) {
    const checked = check();
    if (checked.stderr) process.stderr.write(checked.stderr);
    if (json) { try { checkedFacts = JSON.parse(checked.stdout); } catch { /* Refused subprocess remains an error below. */ } }
    else if (checked.stdout) process.stdout.write(checked.stdout);
    if (checked.error || checked.status !== 0) { const error = Error(`E_UPGRADE: packages pinned to ${version}; post-upgrade check refused. Repair the reported schema/device issues and run check again.`); error.check = checkedFacts; throw error; }
  }
  if (json) console.log(JSON.stringify({ ok: true, version, targets, packageManager: pm, check: checkedFacts, runtimeChanges: history }));
  log(`upgrade: ${targets.join(', ')} pinned to ${version} with ${pm}; commit the lockfile, run check and acceptance tests${targets.includes('snapback4-expo') ? ', and rebuild the native app' : ''}`);
}
try { run(); } catch (error) { if (process.argv.includes('--json')) console.log(JSON.stringify({ ok: false, error: error.message, ...(error.check ? { check: error.check } : {}) })); console.error(error.message); process.exitCode = 1; }
// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000#11-wave-11-runtime-upgrade-history — read installed release history after package-manager symlink replacement.
import { readFileSync, writeFileSync, mkdirSync, renameSync } from 'node:fs';
import { join, dirname } from 'node:path';
import { createRequire } from 'node:module';
import { spawnSync } from 'node:child_process';
const version = value => /^\d+\.\d+\.\d+(?:-[0-9A-Za-z.-]+)?$/.test(value ?? '') ? value : undefined;
export function lockedVersion(text) {
  try {
    const lock = JSON.parse(text);
    const found = version(lock.packages?.['node_modules/snapback4']?.version ?? lock.dependencies?.snapback4?.version);
    if (found) return found;
  } catch { /* Bun lockfiles are JSONC; pnpm is YAML. */ }
  const lines = text.split('\n');
  const found = new Set();
  for (let i = 0; i < lines.length; i++) if (/^\s*['"]?snapback4['"]?:\s*$/.test(lines[i])) {
    for (const line of lines.slice(i + 1, i + 5)) {
      const resolved = /^\s*version:\s*(.+)$/.exec(line)?.[1]?.trim().replace(/^['"]|['"]$/g, '').split('(')[0];
      if (version(resolved)) found.add(resolved);
    }
  }
  for (const match of text.matchAll(/snapback4@([^\s"'/:()[\]{},]+)/g)) if (version(match[1])) found.add(match[1]);
  return found.size === 1 ? [...found][0] : undefined;
}
export function installedVersion(root) {
  try { const require = createRequire(join(root, 'package.json')); return version(JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(dirname(dirname(require.resolve('snapback4/bin-path'))), 'package.json'), 'utf8')).version); } catch { /* Older copied trees may have no installed compiler. */ }
  return undefined;
}
export function previousVersion(root) {
  for (const name of ['runtime-version.json', 'check-surface.json', 'upgrade-version.json']) {
    try { const found = version(JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(root, '.snapback4', name), 'utf8')).version); if (found) return found; } catch { /* absent or invalid */ }
  }
  try { const pkg = JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(root, 'snapback/generated/package.json'), 'utf8')); const found = version(pkg.snapback4Version ?? pkg.version); if (found) return found; } catch { /* absent */ }
  for (const name of ['package-lock.json', 'npm-shrinkwrap.json', 'bun.lock', 'pnpm-lock.yaml']) {
    try { const found = lockedVersion(readFileSync(join(root, name), 'utf8')); if (found) return found; } catch { /* absent */ }
  }
  return installedVersion(root);
}
export function acknowledgeVersion(root, version) {
  const directory = join(root, '.snapback4'); mkdirSync(directory, { recursive: true });
  const path = join(directory, 'runtime-version.json'), temporary = `${path}.${process.pid}.tmp`;
  writeFileSync(temporary, JSON.stringify({ version })); renameSync(temporary, path);
}
export function compareVersions(a, b) {
  const parts = v => { const i = v.indexOf('-'); return i < 0 ? [v, undefined] : [v.slice(0, i), v.slice(i + 1)]; };
  const [ac, ap] = parts(a), [bc, bp] = parts(b);
  const av = ac.split('.').map(Number), bv = bc.split('.').map(Number);
  for (let i = 0; i < 3; i++) if (av[i] !== bv[i]) return av[i] - bv[i];
  if (ap === undefined || bp === undefined) return ap === bp ? 0 : ap === undefined ? 1 : -1;
  const aa = ap.split('.'), ba = bp.split('.');
  for (let i = 0; i < Math.max(aa.length, ba.length); i++) {
    if (aa[i] === ba[i]) continue;
    if (aa[i] === undefined || ba[i] === undefined) return aa[i] === undefined ? -1 : 1;
    const an = /^\d+$/.test(aa[i]), bn = /^\d+$/.test(ba[i]);
    if (an && bn) return Number(aa[i]) - Number(ba[i]);
    if (an !== bn) return an ? -1 : 1;
    return aa[i] < ba[i] ? -1 : 1;
  }
  return 0;
}
export function runtimeChanges(history, previous, installed) {
  if (!version(previous) || !version(installed) || compareVersions(previous, installed) >= 0) return '';
  let selected = false, out = '';
  for (const line of history.split('\n')) {
    if (line.startsWith('## ')) { const v = line.slice(3); selected = !!version(v) && compareVersions(v, previous) > 0 && compareVersions(v, installed) <= 0; }
    if (selected && line.startsWith('## ')) out += (out ? '\n' : '') + line + '\n\n';
    else if (selected && line.startsWith('- ')) out += line + '\n';
  }
  return out ? `runtime changes: ${previous} → ${installed}\n${out}History begins at 0.4.26; earlier changes are not recorded.\n` : '';
}
export function installedHistory(root) {
  // The parent process resolved the old package already. Resolve the new
  // symlink in a fresh process, just like upgrade's post-install check.
  const script = `import {createRequire} from 'node:module';import {join,dirname} from 'node:path';const require=createRequire(join(process.argv[1],'package.json'));console.log(join(dirname(dirname(require.resolve('snapback4/bin-path'))),'CHANGELOG.md'));`;
  const result = spawnSync(process.execPath, ['--input-type=module', '-e', script, root], { encoding: 'utf8' });
  if (result.error || result.status !== 0) throw Error('installed release history unavailable');
  return readFileSync(result.stdout.trim(), 'utf8');
}
//! Recover a version, not invented compiler facts, from an older app.
use super::*;
use std::fs;
// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000#11-wave-11-runtime-upgrade-history — history acknowledgment is independent of compiler qualification.

fn exact(text: &str) -> Option<String> {
    snapback4_lang::release_changes::version(text).map(|_| text.to_owned())
}
fn json_version(text: &str, key: &str) -> Option<String> {
    let value: Json = serde_json::from_str(text).ok()?;
    exact(value[key].as_str()?)
}
pub(super) fn locked_version(text: &str) -> Option<String> {
    if let Ok(lock) = serde_json::from_str::<Json>(text) {
        if let Some(version) = lock.pointer("/packages/node_modules~1snapback4/version").or_else(|| lock.pointer("/dependencies/snapback4/version")).and_then(Json::as_str).and_then(exact) { return Some(version); }
    }
    // Bun's JSONC package tuple; pnpm's importer resolution (not specifier).
    let mut versions = std::collections::BTreeSet::new();
    for (position, _) in text.match_indices("snapback4@") {
        let tail = &text[position + "snapback4@".len()..];
        let candidate = tail.split(|c: char| !(c.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || matches!(c, '.' | '-'))).next().unwrap_or("");
        if let Some(version) = exact(candidate) { versions.insert(version); }
    }
    let lines: Vec<_> = text.lines().collect();
    for (index, line) in lines.iter().enumerate() {
        if line.trim().trim_matches('\'').trim_matches('"') == "snapback4:" {
            for line in lines.iter().skip(index + 1).take(4) {
                if let Some(value) = line.trim().strip_prefix("version:") {
                    let value = value.trim().trim_matches(['\'', '"']).split('(').next().unwrap_or("");
                    if let Some(version) = exact(value) { versions.insert(version); }
                }
            }
        }
    }
    (versions.len() == 1).then(|| versions.into_iter().next()).flatten()
}
pub(super) fn previous(project: &Project) -> Option<String> {
    let read_version = |path, key| fs::read_to_string(path).ok().and_then(|text| json_version(&text, key));
    if let Some(version) = read_version(project.data_dir().join("runtime-version.json"), "version") { return Some(version); }
    if let Some(version) = read_version(project.data_dir().join("check-surface.json"), "version") { return Some(version); }
    if let Some(version) = read_version(project.data_dir().join("upgrade-version.json"), "version") { return Some(version); }
    if let Some(version) = read_version(project.generated_dir().join("package.json"), "snapback4Version") { return Some(version); }
    if let Some(version) = read_version(project.generated_dir().join("package.json"), "version") { return Some(version); }
    for name in ["package-lock.json", "npm-shrinkwrap.json", "bun.lock", "pnpm-lock.yaml"] {
        if let Some(version) = fs::read_to_string(project.root.join(name)).ok().and_then(|text| locked_version(&text)) { return Some(version); }
    }
    None
}
pub(super) fn report(project: &Project, json: bool) {
    if std::env::var("SNAPBACK4_UPGRADE_BASELINE").as_deref() == Ok("1") { return; }
    if let Some(previous) = previous(project) {
        let text = snapback4_lang::release_changes::interval(&previous, env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION"));
        if !text.is_empty() { if json { eprint!("{text}"); } else { print!("{text}"); } }
    }
    let save = || -> std::io::Result<()> {
        fs::create_dir_all(project.data_dir())?;
        let path = project.data_dir().join("runtime-version.json");
        let temporary = path.with_extension(format!("{}.tmp", std::process::id()));
        fs::write(&temporary, serde_json::to_vec(&json!({ "version": env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION") }))?)?;
        fs::rename(temporary, path)
    };
    if let Err(error) = save() { eprintln!("check: runtime history acknowledgment not saved: {error}"); }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    #[test]
    fn lock_resolution_uses_versions_not_dependency_ranges() {
        for text in [r#"{"packages":{"node_modules/snapback4":{"version":"0.4.25"}}}"#, r#"{"dependencies":{"snapback4":{"version":"0.4.25"}}}"#, "{\n\"snapback4\": [\"snapback4@0.4.25\", \"\", {}, \"sha\"],\n}", "importers:\n  .:\n    dependencies:\n      snapback4:\n        specifier: ^0.4.24\n        version: 0.4.25", "packages:\n  snapback4@0.4.25:\n    resolution: {}"] {
            assert_eq!(locked_version(text).as_deref(), Some("0.4.25"), "{text}");
        }
        for text in ["snapback4@latest", "snapback4@0.4.24\nsnapback4@0.4.25", "snapback4:\n  specifier: ^0.4.25", "snapback4@0.4.25.1", "importers:\n  one:\n    snapback4:\n      version: 0.4.25\n  two:\n    snapback4:\n      version: 0.4.27"] { assert!(locked_version(text).is_none(), "{text}"); }
    }
}
//! A compiler upgrade explains the delta against the previous successful check.
// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000#9 — snapshots explain versions; failed compilation cannot replace the old API.
use super::*;
use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};
use std::{fs, path::PathBuf};

#[derive(Serialize, Deserialize, Default)]
struct Surface {
    version: String,
    #[serde(default)] sources: Option<Vec<(String, String)>>,
    api: String,
    notes: BTreeMap<String, usize>,
    refusals: BTreeMap<String, usize>,
}
fn path(project: &Project) -> PathBuf { project.data_dir().join("check-surface.json") }
fn counts(notes: &[snapback4_lang::Diagnostic]) -> BTreeMap<String, usize> {
    let mut counts = BTreeMap::new();
    for note in notes { *counts.entry(note.code.clone()).or_default() += 1; }
    counts
}
fn current(project: &Project, bound: &Bound) -> Surface {
    Surface { version: env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION").into(), sources: project.sources().ok(),
        api: if bound.diagnostics.is_empty() { snapback4_lang::emit::typescript(bound) } else { String::new() },
        notes: counts(&bound.notes), refusals: counts(&bound.diagnostics) }
}
/// The emitter's own fixed layout: row columns and complete operation signatures.
/// Inline object types stay on one line; comments and runtime api objects are excluded.
fn types(api: &str) -> BTreeMap<String, String> {
    let mut scope = Vec::new();
    let mut types = BTreeMap::new();
    for line in api.lines().map(str::trim) {
        if line.starts_with("export const ") || line.starts_with("const operations:") { break; }
        if let Some(name) = line.strip_prefix("export type ").and_then(|s| s.strip_suffix(" = {")) {
            scope.clear(); scope.push(name.to_owned()); continue;
        }
        if line == "};" { scope.pop(); continue; }
        if !matches!(scope.first().map(String::as_str), Some("Rows" | "Ops")) { continue; }
        if let Some(name) = line.strip_suffix(": {") { scope.push(name.trim_matches('"').into()); continue; }
        if line.starts_with('/') { continue; }
        if let Some((name, value)) = line.strip_suffix(';').and_then(|s| s.split_once(": ")) {
            let key = scope.iter().map(String::as_str).chain([name.trim_matches('"')]).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(".");
            if scope[0] == "Ops" {
                if let Some((args, result)) = operation_types(value) {
                    types.insert(format!("{key}.args"), args); types.insert(format!("{key}.result"), result); continue;
                }
            }
            types.insert(key, value.into());
        }
    }
    types
}
fn operation_types(value: &str) -> Option<(String, String)> {
    let body = value.split_once('<')?.1.strip_suffix('>')?;
    let mut depth = 0i32;
    for (index, ch) in body.char_indices() {
        match ch {
            '{' | '[' | '<' | '(' => depth += 1,
            '}' | ']' | '>' | ')' => depth -= 1,
            ',' if depth == 0 => return Some((body[..index].trim().into(), body[index + 1..].trim().into())),
            _ => {},
        }
    }
    None
}

pub(super) fn report(project: &Project, bound: &Bound, json: bool) {
    let old: Surface = match fs::read(path(project)).ok().and_then(|b| serde_json::from_slice(&b).ok()) { Some(old) => old, None => {
        return;
    } };
    let new = current(project, bound);
    if old.version == new.version { return; }
    let mut lines = vec![format!("upgrade delta: {} → {} for snapback/*.q{}", old.version, new.version,
        if old.sources.is_some() && old.sources != new.sources { " (schema also changed)" } else { "" })];
    for (label, before, after) in [("new notes", &old.notes, &new.notes), ("new refusals", &old.refusals, &new.refusals)] {
        let added = after.iter().filter_map(|(code, count)| {
            let delta = count.saturating_sub(*before.get(code).unwrap_or(&0));
            (delta > 0).then(|| format!("{code} +{delta}"))
        }).collect::<Vec<_>>();
        lines.push(format!("  {label}: {}", if added.is_empty() { "none".into() } else { added.join(", ") }));
    }
    if new.api.is_empty() { lines.push("  generated/api.ts: unavailable until the schema compiles; previous types kept".into()); }
    else {
        let before = types(&old.api); let after = types(&new.api);
        let keys: std::collections::BTreeSet<_> = before.keys().chain(after.keys()).collect();
        let mut changed = false;
        for key in keys {
            if before.get(key) == after.get(key) { continue; }
            changed = true;
            lines.push(format!("  generated/api.ts {key}: {} → {}", before.get(key).map(String::as_str).unwrap_or("(added)"), after.get(key).map(String::as_str).unwrap_or("(removed)")));
        }
        if !changed { lines.push("  generated/api.ts types: unchanged".into()); }
    }
    lines.push("  release card: snapback4 guide changes".into());
    for line in lines { if json { eprintln!("{line}"); } else { println!("{line}"); } }
}

pub(super) fn remember(project: &Project, bound: &Bound) {
    if !bound.diagnostics.is_empty() || std::env::var("SNAPBACK4_UPGRADE_BASELINE").as_deref() == Ok("1") { return; }
    let save = || -> std::io::Result<()> {
        fs::create_dir_all(project.data_dir())?;
        let temporary = path(project).with_extension(format!("{}.tmp", std::process::id()));
        fs::write(&temporary, serde_json::to_vec_pretty(&current(project, bound))?)?;
        fs::rename(temporary, path(project))?;
        let _ = fs::remove_file(project.data_dir().join("upgrade-version.json"));
        Ok(())
    };
    if let Err(error) = save() { eprintln!("check: upgrade comparison not saved: {error}"); }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    #[test]
    fn generated_surface_separates_modules_columns_and_nested_types() {
        let api = "export type Rows = {\n  things: {\n    id: string;\n    total: number | null;\n  };\n};\nexport type Ops = {\n  stats: {\n    count: Query<{ room: string; labels: Array<string> }, number | null>;\n  };\n};\nexport const api: Ops = {\n  fake: 42;\n};";
        let types = types(api);
        assert_eq!(types["Rows.things.total"], "number | null");
        assert_eq!(types["Ops.stats.count.result"], "number | null");
        assert_eq!(types.len(), 4);
    }
}

## 10. Expo web-export qualification — 2026-10-14 (EXPOFIX)

Published 0.4.26's Expo starters configured an SVG favicon. Expo's image
pipeline rejects that input during `expo export -p web`, although the
starter-content tests passed. Remove the optional favicon key and unused SVG
from every Expo starter (Todos, chat and social); keep the SVG in Vite's
public directory, where the browser consumes it directly. An application can
supply its own raster favicon. `init --adapt` removes only the exact generated
SVG and its exact setting, preserving custom images, paths and other config.
Fresh UI starters describe the known Todos schema's offline behavior immediately;
`check` must issue no adaptation advice on any untouched starter.

The publication's installed-consumer proof now installs packed CLI/client and
Expo packages, generates the real web and Expo starters, installs their pinned
toolchain dependencies, runs `check` and typecheck, builds Vite, and runs both
`expo export -p web` and the starter's `build:web`. It also adapts each to an
online-only Home schema and repeats the builds, then installs/typechecks/builds
the web-chat, Expo-chat and social templates. Proofs have a twenty-minute
per-distribution guard; individual commands have five minutes. Expo workers
are bounded at two in the direct export and generated build:web scripts;
unbounded worker pools made the namespace-free proof exceed its command guard. Dependency installation can access
the npm registry with prefer-offline caching; Expo's network checks and
telemetry are disabled. This web proof does not qualify native iOS binaries.

`--prepare-client` also packs Expo as a proof input. Linux qualification requires
`--expo <prepared-expo.tgz>` and binds its SRI plus web/Expo/adapt success in
both Ubuntu and Alpine proof records. The publisher checks that SRI against
its preparation and retains those exact Expo bytes in its stage. Darwin-only
publication and resume run the same starter proof. Expo's native release
publication remains the existing separate native qualification path; this
change does not publish an unqualified native package.

FIX28 (2026-10-14) closes the browser gap: the starter-toolchain step also runs
the generated frontend:proof twice for every supported frontend/template pair,
including web --online, and twice after adapting default web/Expo to Home.
These browser attempts run on the qualification host, with occupied first ports
and CPU load, and their complete evidence is required alongside the PROOF
matrix in new Linux receipts. Ubuntu/Alpine remain isolated build/toolchain
consumers (`--build-only`); no host browser or shared libraries enter their
userspaces. Darwin runs the same complete starter/browser step.

The audit covers all emitted assets/config: Expo's app ids, plugins, entrypoint,
Babel/Metro configs, wasm transformer and dependency versions, Vite's module
config, HTML, public SVG and generated offline shell. No other emitted Expo
image key exists. The changed-schema builds exercise journal/device selection,
generated API and adapted Home/sign-in/README paths. Chat/social receive their own
real installs and exports: social has a Router entrypoint and extra plugins.
The audit found its floating Router range selecting an unqualified release
with an unavailable dependency during install; pin Router to 57.0.23, the
version in the existing workspace lock, and qualify it through the same path.


Historical publication stages without a retained Expo proof input are refused
with a specific explanation, preserving all artifacts. A new version closes
the gap; a pre-gap suite receipt cannot authorize publication without the new
real-toolchain evidence. Format-2 stage layout remains readable when it carries
the required Expo bytes; tests distinguish that from genuine old stages.


The command guard uses the existing live command-owner keeper. Only that
keeper signals its own process group; completion and timeout close its private
pipe, and caller death does the same. Commands are asynchronous so npm's
signal handling cannot block the watchdog. Success requires the command's
zero exit plus two empty group-existence observations; lingering members or
inspection errors refuse. Clean userspaces need no host /proc mount. Cleanup
allows up to five seconds beyond the command guard; deliberately detached
processes are outside group custody, as in the existing suite contract.

## 11. Wave 11 runtime upgrade history

Commission and implementer: Charlie Cheever's NARR workstream, Codex,
2026-10-14; target 0.4.28. This amends §9's missing-baseline and changes-card
behavior; it changes neither mutation settlement nor custody. Review together
with LLP 3000.000.000 §11 and LLP 3000.001.000's NARR diagnostics.

A packaged CHANGELOG.md records behavior by release, including client runtime
semantics. The compiler build generates `guide changes` through its own version
from this text. Entries backfill 0.4.26's `useMutation().run()` terminal
settlement (offline waits for reconnect; waitSeconds: 0 returns custody) and
0.4.27's Expo/device fixes and unchanged settlement semantics. History starts
at 0.4.26; an earlier baseline receives all recorded entries with that limit
stated. A future release must add its meaningful runtime changes to this same
file; NARR's 0.4.28 entry covers this lane only, and integration owns the
other lanes' final release entries.

First check prints entries in (previous, installed] before device preflight,
including when schema/device qualification fails. JSON remains one stdout
object; in JSON mode history goes to stderr, and ordinary human output uses stdout. Compiler comparison still requires
its real successful snapshot. A missing compiler snapshot creates no invented
old types/notes/refusals. Version recovery tries the check snapshot, a failed
upgrade's recorded previous version, generated package version metadata, then
npm/Bun/pnpm lockfile resolutions. Ranges are not resolved versions; ambiguous
lockfile versions are unavailable. New generated/package.json includes a
snapback4Version alongside its ESM marker so copied apps retain a baseline.
Failed checks preserve the prior snapshot; successful check remembers the new
compiler surface and removes the temporary upgrade-version record. FIX28 keeps
`.snapback4/runtime-version.json` separately: the last version whose history was
delivered, including on a refused check. History is shown exactly once; a failed
compiler check still retains the successful surface snapshot. The acknowledgment
is atomically written after delivering history (or initializing a missing baseline).
This acknowledgment takes precedence over surface/temporary/generated/lock versions.
Internal upgrade baseline checks suppress history and surface acknowledgment.
An install performed first cannot replace the real older compiler snapshot;
upgrade captures compiler facts only when the installed compiler matches the
acknowledged version. Human upgrade uses human check output; --json returns one
object with package-manager/progress/history output on stderr. Latest or a legacy
positional target cannot downgrade an installed version: only --to <exact version>
authorizes that decision, checked before baseline compilation or install.

Upgrade captures the previous installed package (or lock/generated metadata)
before installation. If old check refuses, it records only that version; new
check can still narrate runtime changes. Without Quarry source upgrade reads
the newly installed package's changelog after symlink replacement and reports
the same interval. It preserves existing package pin/section rules and names
Exact's rebuild command. Rebuild is advice, not an automatic Exact mutation.

A development-only run observer warns once per call after ten continuous
seconds offline while its result is still pending. Reconnect cancels the timer;
another offline interval starts it again until the first warning. Terminal
settlement or rejection clears listeners/timer even after component unmount.
waitSeconds: 0 owns no settlement timer. Development is explicit __DEV__ or NODE_ENV development/test, using the
standard expression bundlers replace. Unknown unbundled browser environments
remain silent; no import.meta syntax or Hermes polyfill is introduced. The warning explains terminal settlement,
reconnect and the custody alternative; it does not cancel or retry a write.

P9 advice is small: a device-planned cursorless page over public unbounded sync,
scoped by a reference outside the sync audience, gets N_SYNC_EMPTY naming the lack of a bounded empty-partition acquisition
proof and a bounded/unfiltered-window or explicit server-client alternative. It changes
no planner/runtime/protocol and does not assert that every empty result stalls;
the analysis verified missing check advice but did not reproduce the runtime
claim. PAGE4 owns planner reach and completeness proofs. Positive and negative
compiler controls bind that distinction; runtime reach is not NARR's claim.

Acceptance: older npm/Bun/pnpm and generated-only baselines, refused old check,
same-version repeat, preflight refusal, exact interval filtering and shared
Rust/JS history text; fake-timer tests for offline threshold/reconnect/production,
terminal cleanup and custody; query-note scoped/bounded/server controls.

NARR readiness: both fresh Codex and Muse mark the same semantic revision
`f70dc90f4d` READY in round 2. Verbatim artifacts, repaired findings and the
scoped verification record are linked from LLP 3000.000.000's NARR readiness
section. Additional review controls change tests only. Round 3 from both families
marks the generic widening wording clarification at 3b770ea426 READY; it changes
no decision logic. No publication or complete-battery claim follows from readiness.


## 12. Wave 11 starter selection and adaptation preview — 2026-10-14

The DX3 fix-list authorization commissions this amendment; no automatic change
to an application's authority is inferred from its old persona fixtures.
A packaged `guide production` worked example is the persona-to-production
transition. It shows password restoration gates, a principal-keyed profile,
a privacy-aware directory, trusted seat enrollment, linked membership creation,
removal of guest smoke helpers, real-account proof and rollback assertions.
An `init --adapt --production` switch is rejected: choosing a directory's
readers or a role's enrollment authority requires application policy.

`init --web --online` selects the small Vite/React starter with
`createApp({ writes: "online", sessionStore: localStorageSessionStore() })`;
its client imports no web device. With the default backend it uses `online only`
tables. With existing Quarry source it keeps the source exactly, even when it
syncs. Transport selection and backend residency are independent. Chat,
social and Expo templates keep their explicit device choices; those forms do
not silently change tiers when combined with `--online`.

`init --adapt` computes and prints the complete file plan before writing:
removal and replacement paths, and edited files that it will preserve.
`--dry-run` always prints without writing. When candidate starter files were
edited, an ordinary invocation defaults to this preview and names `--apply`.
`--apply` applies only recognized unmodified starter files; it never overwrites
custom code or Quarry source. The plan is deterministic for the current files,
# LLP 3000.011.003.000.000: Packaged application proof kit

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Implemented
**Systems:** Snapback 4 proof tooling, starters, fixtures, release source selection
**Author:** Codex for Charlie Cheever
**Date:** 2026-10-14
**Revised:** 2026-10-14
**Related:** [Starters](./3000.011.003.000-offline-ready-application-starters.rfc.md), [Release activation](./3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md), [Built application proof](./3000.011.005.000-qualify-the-built-application.rfc.md)

## 1. Contract

Implementer: PROOF workstream, 2026-10-14. Wave 11 fix list 1 authorizes
one published `snapback4/proof` Node module. It owns orchestration, never client
custody or backend/bundler supervision in the product. Existing `startDev(args)`
and `stopChild(child | {url, stop})` recipes remain usable as module exports.

`startDev`, `startServe(directory)`, `startStatic(directory)` and
`startVite(directory)` return `{url, pid, logs, stop}`. Options carry cwd,
environment, arguments and bounded ready/stop deadlines. Read PORT_LO/PORT_HI
at invocation: absent means an OS-assigned port; exactly one set refuses. With
both set, validate 1 <= LO <= HI <= 65535 and bind the first free port in that
inclusive range; exhaustion refuses. Qualification's occupied first port must
be skipped, never adopted. The existing native SNAPBACK4_READY line already names its direct child PID (LLP 3000.011.004.000); no new readiness log is needed. The listening process binds first and keeps that socket;
never adopt an already-serving port. Vite uses its supported JavaScript server
API in a directly spawned Node child, strict ports, the project's config and
a per-launch identity middleware. The directly spawned frontend calls its
server listen API for each candidate; no probe-and-release step exists. With
no range it calls listen on port 0, which the tested Vite API supports with
strictPort; it reads the actual retained socket address. Static hosting uses a directly spawned Node
child with traversal protection, correct wasm MIME and SPA fallback.

All child stdout/stderr go to separate files beneath `logs/`. Read complete
lines from those files, strip ANSI, bound readiness, and reject premature exit.
Native ready lines must name the directly spawned PID. To assert HTTP identity,
the kit sets a random SNAPBACK4_PROOF_ID only in the child environment;
`/auth/config` includes `proof_id` only when this variable is nonempty. It
confers no authority or bypass, and ordinary servers omit it. Both static and Vite frontend child
responses carry an `x-snapback4-proof` marker. Identity validation
precedes returning a handle. No public development door is enabled in serve.

Register every spawned child and browser process for an exit-time SIGKILL
sweep, using retained native ChildProcess handles, not process-name kills.
TERM followed by bounded KILL/reap stops a child. Every cleanup action has
its own bound and later actions still run when an earlier action fails.
`cli(args)` runs the native binary, awaits close, returns separate stdout and
stderr, and has a command deadline. It never runs npm/npx or shell strings.

`withBrowser({executablePath: process.env.CHROME}, callback)` launches Chromium
through playwright-core, closes with a bound, and registers its actual browser
process. Root needs --no-sandbox; other hosts first attempt a sandboxed launch
and retry without it only on recognized sandbox-unavailable diagnostics: "No usable sandbox", "Running as
root without --no-sandbox", "Failed to move to new namespace", "SUID sandbox",
"sandbox ... Operation not permitted", or "Failed to unshare ... Operation not
permitted". Unrelated failures never retry.
`waitForFresh` polls for nonempty changed text; `fillRN` clears and types real
keystrokes; `statusText` uses text selectors; `selectByValue` selects stable
option values. The browser executable is CHROME (when provided), undefined executablePath is omitted from launch options, then Playwright's
installed Chromium is used; an absent executable refuses at launch, with its path.
waitForFresh defaults to 30 seconds. Binary resolution is resolveBinary from
the same installed snapback4 package (override SNAPBACK4_BIN only for explicit
workspace tests); the opt-in identity requires CLI/client 0.4.28 or newer and
an older CLI omits proof_id and therefore fails the exact marker assertion; no inferred version comparison or success claim is made. cli(args) does not
invoke package managers; publication uses its existing guarded command runner.

None interprets an HTTP acknowledgement as rendered settlement.

## 2. Explicit source and aliases

`deploy dir --schema file` compiles exactly that saved Quarry file and stages
those same bytes as canonical snapback/schema.q, preserving operation names
regardless of the saved file's name. Compile diagnostics name the selected input.
This happens without swapping the working schema or generating its types.
It does not concatenate other snapback/*.q files. It allocates ordinary numeric
immutable releases, uses the same .pending-adoption marker, transaction/history,
latest selection, restoration-intent preview and no-op checks as normal deploy;
no alternate activation path or restoration bypass exists. The normal deployment path
retains multi-file behavior and activation semantics. Server/native companion source comes from the current project, while all
schema-dependent generated signatures and native builds run against the selected
schema in a private snapshot. A uniquely created snapshot project has
snapback/schema.q, captured companion source, fresh snapback/generated/
and copied native sources (target caches excluded). Host-installed node_modules
is linked into the private project; package.json preserves host module policy.
The snapshot is RAII-owned and removed on success or error; nothing is executed
through the working generated types. Its server entry is typechecked against
the fresh generated server signature before activation; its native crate builds
against fresh generated native.rs into its own native-target directory. Never reuse the working generated signature or
write selected-schema types there. Package prepared signatures and native bytes
with the captured saved source; retain the original host package installation
for dependency resolution. Standalone means no Project::locate result from the current directory/ancestors; that saved source has no companion app. Native snapshot manifests preserve their source layout and rebase only Cargo
dependency paths against the original native crate's directory. TOML parsing
covers normal, build/dev, target-specific, patch/replace and local workspace
dependency maps. Absolute paths stay equivalent; missing dependencies refuse
before compilation. Never rewrite the package's own lib/bin paths to the
working tree; those must compile against the selected private generated types.
 Missing/stale external server
import graphs fail private checking with an actionable TypeScript diagnostic. Keep saved variants outside snapback/ to avoid normal
multi-file compilation. Rollback selects an existing immutable release, never
reads the current schema. The proof `deploy` helper requires a schema path;
`rollback` requires an explicit target; `testSource` requires a source path.
`test --source file` uses the selected bytes in a memory engine (optionally
with fixtures), so it cannot adopt a working store. Diagnostics name the file.

Aliases affect only test.cases.jsonl fixture arguments. Protocol request decoding never sees this saved-name map and accepts all ordinary literal data unchanged. Saved aliases remain explicit `$name` / `$name.field`. A bare string matching
an earlier save alias refuses with the alias name and `$name` rewrite, before
wire decoding; ordinary strings remain literals. This avoids ambiguous auto
substitution into text fields. Unknown `$name` refuses by exact name lookup, never fuzzy matching.
`$name.field` requires every field in the saved object, refusing by the missing
field's name (a non-record has no fields). A `$` appearing inside ordinary text
is literal. `$$text` escapes to the literal `$text`; `{"$literal": value}`
in a fixture argument escapes any value, including an exact bare alias name. This affects only fixture case argument substitution, not protocol decoding.
Fixture preflight/sweep excludes explicitly saved arguments as before.

## 3. Starter and qualification

Web/Expo init emits frontend:proof and day2:proof (the latter takes two saved
source paths as arguments); both use the kit. The additional command(binary,
args) helper applies cli's same direct-child/log/deadline contract to external
build/test tools, without invoking a shell. Web/Expo init emits frontend:proof on the kit and declares playwright-core.
The already-shipped ?api= override is not introduced or expanded here. Proofs
use it on an owned loopback frontend and fresh browser context; production URL
policy belongs to the existing client/starter surface. The user explicitly
assigns source flags, fixture aliases, frontend proofs and their manifest entries
to PROOF; the two lane-owned text areas below remain separate.

The proof starts a memory dev and built static output, passes the actual URL
as ?api=, signs in through the generated screen and drives a visible form when
the default todos schema is present. The fresh terminal receipt precedes the
rendered row assertion. Adapt writes missing proof files and manifest entries (no package-manager command) and keeps
application-owned proof files/scripts. Runtime ranges are never baked from
the initializer's environment; explicit init --port remains a deliberate
client/interactive default, separate from the kit's runtime URL.

Linux publication qualification runs frontend:proof twice for every supported
frontend/template pair: web Todos, web Todos --online, Expo Todos, web chat,
Expo chat and Expo social. Every attempt uses an occupied first port and bounded
CPU load. Retain all twelve exits, log paths and runtime ranges in its receipt; any failed attempt fails qualification. Test
occupied/exhausted ranges, wrong identities, ANSI/blank ready lines, early death,
command close/drained output, browser flags, cleanup escalation, exit sweeps,
saved-source isolation and alias refusals. Three copied wave-11 proofs (018-day2-cross-country-team-splits, 020-day2-golf-tee-time-lottery and 057-service-status-page, copied from ~/sb4-loop/wave11/apps into /tmp/sb4-w11-proof-lanes) retain
their application assertions while replacing orchestration; record line counts
and execution results. Guide testing replaces orchestration recipes with the
kit and four rules: file logs; fresh signals; receipts outside unmounting
components; HTTP acknowledgement is not React settlement.

NARR owns deploy/rollback narration; DX3 owns general client recipes and discovery.

## 4. Custody scope and review disposition

The registered process set uses retained handles. Static and Vite Node hosts
start in private Unix groups and launch a live Node keeper in that same group.
The host's stdin is a driver-owned liveness pipe; driver death including SIGKILL
produces EOF and terminates the host. The keeper's stdin belongs only to the
host. Host exit (including SIGKILL)
closes it; the keeper signals its own group with SIGKILL. The driver gives the host only the read end of its liveness pipe; the keeper
likewise receives only its pipe's read end. Parent write ends are close-on-exec
and never appear in child stdio or IPC descriptors; compiler stdin is its own
pipe, never these write ends. Thus descendant inheritance cannot retain a
liveness writer after its owner dies. The keeper's live membership
pins the group identity, so no observer signals a recycled negative PID.
Vite closes its compiler before normal host exit; the keeper sweeps anything
remaining after either normal or forced exit. On Windows, direct-child cleanup
remains supported; private Unix-group custody is not claimed. Browser processes
use Playwright's process ownership and close before forced stop. Native servers use their existing SNAPBACK4_SUPERVISOR_PID parent-death contract
with the proof driver's PID and already own worker cleanup. Arbitrary detached application processes are outside
this module's supported custody. Publication's existing live-keeper groups remain
the outer command owner and enforce drained groups after each proof, including
abrupt driver death. No process-name or unrelated PID kills are allowed.

## 5. Exact helper defaults

Each launch uses an independent crypto.randomUUID marker. Native children receive
that marker as SNAPBACK4_PROOF_ID; the parent requires exact equality in
/auth/config.proof_id. Frontend children receive their launch UUID, and the
parent requires the exact pid:UUID header, never mere presence. Children in a
scenario need not share a marker: independent markers prove each handle's owner.
Native hosts receive --port LO-HI (or --port 0); only the child binds and prints
its actual loopback URL. The frontend child binds internally, never by a dropped
probe. Defaults: ready 30 s; TERM grace 5 s; KILL reap 5 s; CLI/command 120 s;
each cleanup 10 s; locator/navigation/fresh signal 30 s; browser/context close
5 s each. Log files have fresh UUID names below cwd/logs, never reused.

startServe serves an immutable deployed backend directory; startStatic serves
built frontend files, not a database. command uses a caller-selected executable
and argv (normal spawn PATH lookup when not absolute), shell:false semantics,
file logs and close/deadline checks; cli uses the package resolver. Use direct
tool binaries, never npm/npx to launch proof servers. Standard starter proof
reads identity/personas from its owned dev at run time, chooses guest/password/
persona sign-in and proves the visible todo add when Task exists. With a custom
Home, it proves sign-in/readiness only; the emitted instructions require the
builder's own business assertions after replacing the screen.

Fixture substitution recursively visits JSON arrays/object values. Alias lookup
is exact and case-sensitive. A $ prefix parses the name before the first dot,
then traverses each exact field; no dot returns a saved row's id. $$ strips one
leading dollar and is terminal (no re-expansion). A sole-key {"$literal":value}
is terminal and returns value untouched. Bare fixture strings matching the saved
map refuse; the wire decoder never receives or consults that map. All other
strings, including a dollar substring, stay literal. Normal Vite/compiler
children remain in the host's private group and receive separate stdin; daemonized
processes leaving it are outside custody. The keeper handles EOF with immediate
SIGKILL of its own group; the host handles driver EOF with immediate exit. Parent
write descriptors never pass to either child, so descendants cannot hold them.
Windows has direct-child stop/exit cleanup only; no abrupt-death group guarantee.

## 6. Source and discovery boundaries

Node hosts log exactly SNAPBACK4_PROOF_READY followed by JSON with kind, url
and pid; the parent reads complete ANSI-stripped lines from that launch's files,
requires the directly spawned host PID, then asserts its exact pid:UUID HTTP
header. The handle's pid is that same host PID. Vite's inline configuration
forces loopback, the selected port and strictPort:true over project settings;
the child tries each range candidate through listen, keeping the successful
socket. Selection uses {...process.env, ...options.env} at each invocation:
explicit env entries override the process environment and nothing writes back
to it. Browser undefined executablePath is omitted; a selected missing path
refuses with that path, never switches browsers; absent Playwright Chromium
also refuses. Sandbox diagnostic matching is case-insensitive on only the
listed patterns.

Save names are nonempty and contain no dot or leading dollar; ambiguous names
refuse at fixture validation before any case executes. Fixture object keys are
never substituted. $name with no field returns the
saved record's string id when present; otherwise it returns the saved scalar,
array or object unchanged. $name.field traverses object values and refuses
missing fields by name. Sole-key $literal and $$ escapes are terminal.
The CLI never writes saved variants into snapback; normal compile still reads
all snapback/*.q exactly as before. The explicit source flag selects one complete
backend even if it lives there, including the ordinary schema.q. Isolation here
means preserving working source/generated files and using private preparation,
not preventing a builder from manually placing another module in a watched
directory. Outside-snapback archive placement remains the documented recipe.

The private native tree includes its nested crates and standalone workspace.
Every copied Cargo.toml rebases dependency paths; internal dependencies point
to copied private crates, external SDK dependencies to their original installation.
Package source paths stay private. Workspace-inherited packages without a complete
standalone workspace under snapback/native refuse before compilation with the
standalone-workspace recipe. A missing member/dependency refuses normally from
the private build without affecting working files. RAII cleans snapshots on
ordinary errors/success; abrupt SIGKILL can leave a private scratch directory,
which is not a serving process or adopted release. No crash-time filesystem
cleanup claim is made.

Implemented in snapback4/proof, starter generators and explicit-source verbs.
Final independent [Muse](./reviews/3000.011.003.000.000-proof-r8.muse.md) and
[fresh Codex](./reviews/3000.011.003.000.000-proof-r8.codex.md) both returned READY
on the complete contract above. Earlier reviews and exact briefs remain verbatim.
The source-companion and driver-SIGKILL findings became regressions;
remaining rounds clarified existing scope and helper contracts without widening
normal source discovery or protocol decoding.

Evidence: [line counts](../snapback4/reports/20261014-proof-kit/line-counts.json),
[the four loaded-host attempts](../snapback4/reports/20261014-proof-kit/frontend-proofs.json)
and the workstream report record scoped validation, inherited root-gate
preconditions, and publication limits. This work does not publish a release.

Acceptance: v1/v2/rollback/refused-v3/latest and explicit refused selection;
non-numeric-history rollback; deploy preview state equality; empty/populated
warnings; all packaged rollback claims; old release E_OP/NOT_FOUND/null and exact
forward values; retained captures after widening plus deleted/reused parent and
new-row refusal; service-token job completion and unauthorized/read-only requests;
stale-lock recovery and live-owner conflict provenance. HS owns handshake/types;
DX2 owns starters/client recipes; DIAG and PAGE2 own language/planner diagnostics.


REL2 review round one: fresh GPT-6.1-sol found legacy predecessor selection,
pre-dormancy constraint narration and omitted static worker prerequisite checks.
All three were reproduced and corrected with migration, unique/narrowing and
worker-selection regressions. Review artifacts retain the verbatim findings.


REL2 round two: [Muse](./reviews/3000.000.000-rel2-wave10-r2.muse.md) and
[fresh Codex](./reviews/3000.000.000-rel2-wave10-r2.codex.md) both READY on

## 11. Wave 11 factual release narration

Commission and implementer: Charlie Cheever's NARR workstream, Codex,
2026-10-14; target 0.4.28. This amends §§9.2 and 10's narration, not retention
or admission policy. Independent readiness reviews cover this section together
with LLP 3000.011.003.000 §11 and LLP 3000.001.000's NARR diagnostics.

Compatibility is directional. A narrowing warning counts active row images
whose named column value fails the candidate's same type/text/asset predicate
used in narrowed-row activation; it does not count table population or
reference-closed dependents. Literal defaults and optional nulls are materialized
under the current schema. Values are bounded, de-duplicated samples. Missing
required fields remain a backfill refusal, not a dormant-value warning.
Zero incompatible values produce a note without a population count. Deploy
widening describes values future writes could use and the previous release
that cannot hold them; forward selection of saved source emits no widening
warning. Counts describe the read snapshot at narration time; concurrent
writes can change a later activation. Preview still performs exact activation
on its coherent private snapshot and changes no live state.

Scheduled behavior compares the schedule definition and lexical authored job
source, excluding comments, whitespace and source positions, before comparing
compiled programs. Missing job/schedule, edited schedule and edited job body
are distinct warnings. An unchanged body whose compiled program changes gets
a dependency note naming changed read/written tables and added/removed columns
when available. Missing saved source does not justify claiming a job absent.
Rollback preview also lists operations absent in the candidate and current
rule clauses differing from its rule. These are behavior differences to review,
not claims that every changed rule weakens enforcement. Removed table populations
are named without guessing whether a generic table is a hold/safeguard.

Receipts report total current dormant rows (whole-table and narrowed), cells
and logical index entries separately, and narrowed restorations separately.
Samples are sorted, de-duplicated and labelled by kind/path, at most five ids
per path. FIX28 makes row and cell populations disjoint: whole-table/narrowed dormant
rows include any separately retained cells of those same ids. Column counts and
samples include only retained cells of still-active rows. Covered cell bytes and
assets belong to their dormant row item; definitions with zero standalone cells
remain inventoried for restoration. Index entries are an independent logical
unit. The archived-row rollback example is one row and two cells, with disjoint
sample ids. Restoration counters still record actual consumed cells, including
covered cells, independently of the current inventory counts.
Retained indexes are definitions, not physical dormant keys; inventory computes
the entries those definitions represent over current/retained images, including
word-token fanout and dormant cells, rather than labelling row population as
entries. The existing inventory JSON field `rows` retains its count slot;
`kind` determines its unit. Human column/index previews use cells/entries.

Graceful shutdown unlinks only the address file inode this host opened, after
release-control drains, while retaining its ownership sidecar and lock. Owner
drop also clears its own address inode. A replaced address record is preserved.
Dead-owner reclaim is silent normally and a note under --verbose, without an
old URL that could be mistaken for new readiness. Busy live ownership still
refuses with PID/address/directory. Fatal signals remain immediate.

The deploy card distinguishes bare-null point queries, explicit `! else CODE`
guards (including queries), ignored added columns and compatible forward.
Its rollback client recipe pairs generated types with selected server source,
or requires runtime field/operation compatibility handling in a shared client.
Preview names behavior differences beside dormant counts because storage
restoration cannot restore product checks missing from selected source.

Acceptance: one CLI/HTTP regression seeds three draft rows, widens an enum,
adds a literal-default column to a scheduled sweep, sets one archived value,
SIGTERMs serve, previews/rolls back and forwards. Assert exact warning/note,
kind counts, labelled samples, old-source field/guard behavior and lock cleanup;
check the guide's matching text. Separate controls cover actual job/schedule
changes, missing operations/rules, words-index fanout, empty incompatible
populations, verbose dead reclaim and preserved live ownership.

+
+#[test]
+fn every_frontend_template_documents_proofs_and_expo_uses_the_runtime_api() {
+    for args in [vec!["init", "--web"], vec!["init", "--web", "--online"], vec!["init", "--expo"], vec!["init", "--web", "--template", "chat"], vec!["init", "--expo", "--template", "chat"], vec!["init", "--template", "social"]] {
+        let dir = Project::new(); ok(dir.run(&args));
+        let readme = dir.text("README.md");
+        for word in ["frontend:proof", "day2:proof", "PORT_LO", "PORT_HI", "CHROME"] { assert!(readme.contains(word), "{args:?}: missing {word}"); }
+        let package: serde_json::Value = serde_json::from_str(&dir.text("package.json")).unwrap();
+        assert_eq!(package["scripts"]["frontend:proof"], "node scripts/frontend-proof.mjs");
+        if args.contains(&"--web") { assert!(dir.text("src/client.ts").contains("new URLSearchParams(window.location.search).get(\"api\")")); }
+        else {
+            assert!(dir.text("src/client.ts").contains("Platform.OS === \"web\" ? new URLSearchParams(window.location.search).get(\"api\") : null"));
+            assert_eq!(package["scripts"]["native:smoke"], "node scripts/launch.mjs native-smoke");
+        }
+    }
+}

   assert.match(result.stdout, /runtime changes: 0\.4\.25 →/); assert.match(result.stdout, /run\(\).*sent \| failed/);
 });
+
+// The installed package facade changes version as npm would; the current check
+// runs the actual binary, so acknowledgment and surface writes are real.
+function upgradeFixture(t, installed = releaseVersion, latest = releaseVersion) {
+  const dir = project(t, 'fix28-upgrade-'); mkdirSync(join(dir, 'snapback'));
+  writeFileSync(join(dir, 'snapback/schema.q'), aggregates); success(invoke(['check'], dir));
+  const surface = join(dir, '.snapback4/check-surface.json');
+  writeFileSync(surface, JSON.stringify({ ...JSON.parse(readFileSync(surface)), version: '0.4.27' }));
+  writeFileSync(join(dir, '.snapback4/runtime-version.json'), '{"version":"0.4.27"}');
+  const pack = join(dir, 'node_modules/snapback4'); mkdirSync(join(pack, 'dist'), { recursive: true }); mkdirSync(join(pack, 'bin'));
+  writeFileSync(join(pack, 'dist/bin-path.js'), '');
+  writeFileSync(join(pack, 'package.json'), JSON.stringify({ name: 'snapback4', version: installed, exports: { './bin-path': './dist/bin-path.js' } }));
+  writeFileSync(join(pack, 'CHANGELOG.md'), readFileSync(new URL('../CHANGELOG.md', import.meta.url)));
+  writeFileSync(join(pack, 'bin/snapback4.js'), `const r=require('child_process').spawnSync(${JSON.stringify(bin)},process.argv.slice(2),{encoding:'utf8'});process.stdout.write(r.stdout);process.stderr.write(r.stderr);process.exit(r.status);`);
+  writeFileSync(join(dir, 'package.json'), JSON.stringify({ dependencies: { snapback4: installed }, packageManager: 'npm@10' }));
+  mkdirSync(join(dir, 'tools')); writeFileSync(join(dir, 'tools/npm'), `#!${process.execPath}
+const fs=require('fs');if(process.argv[2]==='view')console.log('${JSON.stringify(latest)}');else{fs.appendFileSync('installs','install\\n');const v=process.argv.at(-1).split('@').at(-1);for(const file of ['package.json','node_modules/snapback4/package.json']){const p=JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(file));if(p.dependencies)p.dependencies.snapback4=v;else p.version=v;fs.writeFileSync(file,JSON.stringify(p));}}`, { mode: 0o755 });
+  return { dir, env: { PATH: `${join(dir, 'tools')}:${process.env.PATH}` } };
+}
+for (const order of ['upgrade-first', 'install-then-upgrade', 'install-then-check']) for (const json of [false, true]) test(`runtime history is acknowledged once: ${order}, ${json ? 'JSON' : 'human'}`, t => {
+  const { dir, env } = upgradeFixture(t, order === 'upgrade-first' ? '0.4.27' : releaseVersion);
+  const first = invoke([order === 'install-then-check' ? 'check' : 'upgrade', ...(json ? ['--json'] : [])], dir, env); success(first);
+  const stream = first.stdout + first.stderr;
+  assert.equal((stream.match(/runtime changes: 0\.4\.27 →/g) ?? []).length, 1, stream);
+  if (json) assert.equal(JSON.parse(first.stdout).ok, true);
+  else { assert.doesNotMatch(first.stdout, /"problems"\s*:/); assert.match(first.stdout, /check:/); }
+  const again = invoke(['check'], dir, env); success(again);
+  const upgrade = invoke(['upgrade'], dir, env); success(upgrade);
+  assert.doesNotMatch(again.stdout + again.stderr + upgrade.stdout + upgrade.stderr, /runtime changes:/);
+  assert.equal(JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(dir, '.snapback4/runtime-version.json'))).version, releaseVersion);
+});
+test('registry latest cannot silently downgrade; only --to authorizes it', t => {
+  const { dir, env } = upgradeFixture(t, releaseVersion, '0.4.26');
+  const before = readFileSync(join(dir, '.snapback4/check-surface.json'), 'utf8');
+  for (const args of [['upgrade'], ['upgrade', '0.4.26']]) {
+    const refused = invoke(args, dir, env); assert.equal(refused.status, 1); assert.match(refused.stderr, /older than installed.*refusing a downgrade.*--to 0.4.26/);
+    assert.equal(readFileSync(join(dir, '.snapback4/check-surface.json'), 'utf8'), before);
+    assert.equal(readFileSync(join(dir, 'package.json'), 'utf8').includes(releaseVersion), true);
+  }
+  success(invoke(['upgrade', '--to', '0.4.26'], dir, env));
+  assert.equal(JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(dir, 'package.json'))).dependencies.snapback4, '0.4.26');
+});

+});

// Runtime launcher: copied apps read their current owner's range on every run.
import { spawn } from "node:child_process";
import { dirname, join } from "node:path";
import { createRequire } from "node:module";
import { resolveBinary } from "snapback4/bin-path";
import { portRange, stopChild } from "snapback4/proof";
const require = createRequire(new URL("../package.json", import.meta.url));
const mode = process.argv[2];
const range = portRange();
const low = range === "0" ? null : Number(range.split("-")[0]);
const high = range === "0" ? null : Number(range.split("-")[1]);
if (low !== null && high === low && !["backend", "native-smoke"].includes(mode)) throw Error("the starter needs two runtime ports");
const port = ["backend", "native-smoke"].includes(mode) ? low === null ? BACKEND : low : low === null ? FRONTEND : low + 1;
const command = ["backend", "native-smoke"].includes(mode) ? resolveBinary() : process.execPath;
const args = mode === "native-smoke" ? ["witness", "ios", "--build", ".", "--smoke", ...(low === null ? [] : ["--port", String(port)])]
  : mode === "backend" ? ["dev", "--port", String(port)]
  : mode.startsWith("vite") ? [join(dirname(require.resolve("vite/package.json")), "bin/vite.js"), ...(mode === "vite-preview" ? ["preview"] : []), "--port", String(port), "--strictPort"]
  : [join(dirname(require.resolve("expo/package.json")), "bin/cli"), "start", ...(mode === "expo-web" ? ["--web"] : []), "--port", String(port)];
const env = { ...process.env };
if (low !== null) {
  env.VITE_SNAPBACK_URL = `http://127.0.0.1:${low}`;
  env.EXPO_PUBLIC_SNAPBACK_URL = env.VITE_SNAPBACK_URL;
}
const child = spawn(command, args, { stdio: "inherit", env });
process.once("exit", () => child.kill("SIGKILL"));
for (const signal of ["SIGTERM", "SIGINT"]) process.once(signal, () => {
  stopChild(child).then(() => process.exit(0), error => { console.error(error); process.exit(1); });
});
child.on("error", error => { console.error(error); process.exitCode = 1; });
child.once("close", code => { process.exitCode = code ?? 1; });
// Build first: npm run build (web) or npm run build:web (Expo web).
// Replace the application assertions when replacing the starter screen.
import assert from "node:assert/strict";
import { randomUUID } from "node:crypto";
import { startDev, startStatic, withBrowser, fillRN, statusText, waitForFresh, bounded, cleanup } from "snapback4/proof";

const dev = await startDev();
let web;
try {
  const identity = await (await fetch(`${dev.url}/auth/config`, { signal: AbortSignal.timeout(5000) })).json();
  const personas = (await (await fetch(`${dev.url}/dev/status`, { signal: AbortSignal.timeout(5000) })).json()).personas;
  web = await startStatic("dist");
  await withBrowser({ executablePath: process.env.CHROME }, async context => {
    const page = await context.newPage();
    await page.goto(`${web.url}/?api=${encodeURIComponent(dev.url)}&witness=web`, { waitUntil: "domcontentloaded" });
    if (identity.guests) {
      await page.getByRole("button", { name: "Continue as a guest", exact: true }).click();
    } else if (identity.password) {
      await fillRN(page.getByLabel("Email", { exact: true }), `proof-${randomUUID()}@example.test`);
      await fillRN(page.getByLabel("Password", { exact: true }), "Proof-password-928!");
      await page.getByRole("button", { name: "Sign up", exact: true }).click();
    } else {
      assert.ok(personas.length, "declare identity or personas before proving the starter");
      await page.getByRole("button", { name: personas[0], exact: true }).click();
    }
    await page.getByRole("button", { name: /^(Sign out|Switch persona)$/, exact: true }).waitFor();
    const task = page.getByLabel("Task", { exact: true });
    if (await task.count()) {
      const title = `Proof ${randomUUID()}`;
      await fillRN(task, title);
      // Arm the app's SDK receipt outside the form, which may unmount.
      const button = page.getByRole("button", { name: "Add", exact: true });
      await button.evaluate((element, title) => window.__snapback4Witness.beginForm("add", { title }, element), title);
      await button.click();
      const receipt = await bounded(page.evaluate(() => window.__snapback4Witness.formResult()), 30_000, "clicked write receipt");
      assert.equal(receipt.state, "sent", JSON.stringify(receipt));
      await waitForFresh(statusText(page, new RegExp(title)), null);
    }
  });
  console.log("frontend:proof passed");
} finally { await cleanup(() => web?.stop(), () => dev.stop()); }
