#!/usr/bin/env node
// `npx snapback4 <verb>`: the Rust binary, found in the platform package
// installed beside this one, or named by SNAPBACK4_BIN, or built in this
// repository. The JavaScript here only finds and runs it, and the two live and
// die together: SIGINT, SIGTERM and SIGHUP to this wrapper are forwarded, and
// SIGKILL, which cannot be forwarded, still stops the binary: it is told this
// wrapper's PID (SNAPBACK4_SUPERVISOR_PID) and exits when its parent is gone
// (Linux: the kernel's parent-death signal; elsewhere within 250 ms). So a
// recorded PID of either stops the server; `GET /dev/status` reports both
// (`pid` is the binary, `wrapperPid` this wrapper), and so does the stderr line
// `SNAPBACK4_READY … url=… pid=… wrapperPid=…`.
import { spawn } from "node:child_process";
import { resolveBinary } from "../dist/resolve-binary.js";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
import { constants } from "node:os";

if (process.argv[2] === "web-shell") {
  try { await (await import("./web-shell.js")).runWebShell(process.argv.slice(3)); }
  catch (error) { console.error(`E_INPUT at web-shell: ${error.message}\n  rewrite: export a static app, then run snapback4 web-shell dist --base /`); process.exit(2); }
  process.exit(0);
}

let binary;
try { binary = resolveBinary(); }
catch (error) { console.error(error.message); process.exit(2); }
// Internal export helpers share the wrapper's kernel lease through fd 3.
const exportLease = process.argv[2] === "witness" && ["export-lease", "export-publish"].includes(process.argv[3]);
const child = spawn(binary, process.argv.slice(2), { stdio: exportLease ? ["inherit", "inherit", "inherit", 3] : "inherit", env: {
  ...process.env, SNAPBACK4_ACQUIRE_HELPER: fileURLToPath(new URL("./witness-acquire.js", import.meta.url)),
  SNAPBACK4_UPGRADE_HELPER: fileURLToPath(new URL("./upgrade.js", import.meta.url)),
  SNAPBACK4_WEB_HELPER: fileURLToPath(new URL("./witness-web.js", import.meta.url)),
  SNAPBACK4_SUPERVISOR_PID: String(process.pid),
} });
const handlers = new Map(["SIGINT", "SIGTERM", "SIGHUP"].map(signal => [signal, () => { try { child.kill(signal); } catch { /* gone */ } }]));
for (const [signal, handler] of handlers) process.on(signal, handler);
// `npx snapback4 dev` puts npm and `sh -c` above this wrapper. A SIGTERM to the
// npx PID kills npm and the shell but never reaches us, so the server would keep
// serving as an orphan. Our parent's death is the stop signal. (`process.ppid` is
// read once at startup, so probe the original parent instead.)
const parent = process.ppid;
const parentAlive = () => { try { process.kill(parent, 0); return true; } catch (error) { return error.code === "EPERM"; } };
const orphanWatch = parent > 1 ? setInterval(() => {
  if (!parentAlive()) { try { child.kill("SIGTERM"); } catch { /* gone */ } }
}, 250) : null;
orphanWatch?.unref();
child.on("exit", (code, signal) => {
  if (orphanWatch) clearInterval(orphanWatch);
  for (const [name, handler] of handlers) process.off(name, handler);
  if (signal) {
    // Restore the default disposition before re-raising. Some platforms do not
    // terminate on this call; never let their empty event loop report success.
    const status = 128 + (constants.signals[signal] ?? { SIGHUP: 1, SIGINT: 2, SIGTERM: 15 }[signal] ?? 1);
    setTimeout(() => process.exit(status), 100);
    try { process.kill(process.pid, signal); } catch { process.exit(status); }
    return;
  }
  process.exit(code ?? 1);
});
//! Timings belong to a successful activation, never to an uncommitted candidate.
use std::time::{Duration, Instant};

#[derive(Default, Debug)]
pub struct AdoptionReport {
    pub changes:Vec<String>,
    pub phases: Vec<(&'static str, Duration)>,
    /// Nested attribution, not additional time to add to `phases`.
    pub details: Vec<(String, Duration)>,
    pub views: Vec<ViewBuild>,
}

#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct ViewBuild {
    pub name: String,
    /// Source row visits; a row-keyed rebuild can visit one source per member.
    pub rows: u64,
    pub elapsed: Duration,
}

impl AdoptionReport {
    // @ref LLP 3000.000.000#11-wave-11-factual-release-narration — current dormant storage is counted by unit; samples never mix units.
    pub fn dormant(items: &[super::release_history::DormantItem]) -> String {
        let mut counts = [0u64; 3];
        let mut samples = std::collections::BTreeMap::<String, std::collections::BTreeSet<String>>::new();
        for item in items {
            let (slot, label) = match item.kind.as_str() {
                "column" => (1, format!("cells {}", item.path)),
                "index" => (2, format!("entries {}", item.path)),
                _ => (0, format!("rows {}", item.path)),
            };
            counts[slot] += item.rows;
            if !item.sample_ids.is_empty() { samples.entry(label).or_default().extend(item.sample_ids.iter().cloned()); }
        }
        let labelled = samples.into_iter().map(|(label, ids)| format!("{label} [{}]", ids.into_iter().take(5).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", "))).collect::<Vec<_>>();
        format!("{} {}, {} {}, {} index {} dormant{}", counts[0], if counts[0] == 1 { "row" } else { "rows" }, counts[1], if counts[1] == 1 { "cell" } else { "cells" }, counts[2], if counts[2] == 1 { "entry" } else { "entries" },
            if labelled.is_empty() { String::new() } else { format!("; samples: {}", labelled.join("; ")) })
    }
    pub fn compact(&self)->String {
        let elapsed:Duration=self.phases.iter().map(|(_,d)|*d).sum();
        let mut changes=self.changes.iter().take(5).cloned().collect::<Vec<_>>();
        if self.changes.len()>5 {changes.push(format!("{} more changes",self.changes.len()-5));}
        format!("{}; {:.1} ms",if changes.is_empty(){"adopted".into()}else{changes.join(", ")},elapsed.as_secs_f64()*1000.0)
    }
    pub fn summary(&self) -> String {
        let report = self;
        let mut out = String::new();
        if !report.phases.is_empty() {
            out.push_str(&format!(
                "; phases [{}]",
                report
                    .phases
                    .iter()
                    .map(|(name, time)| format!("{name} {:.3} ms", time.as_secs_f64() * 1000.0))
                    .collect::<Vec<_>>()
                    .join(", ")
            ));
        }
        if !report.details.is_empty() {
            out.push_str(&format!(
                "; phase details (nested) [{}]",
                report
                    .details
                    .iter()
                    .map(|(name, time)| format!("{name} {:.3} ms", time.as_secs_f64() * 1000.0))
                    .collect::<Vec<_>>()
                    .join(", ")
            ));
        }
        if !report.views.is_empty() {
            out.push_str(&format!(
                "; rebuilt {}",
                report
                    .views
                    .iter()
                    .map(|view| format!(
                        "{} ({} source visits, {:.3} ms)",
                        view.name,
                        view.rows,
                        view.elapsed.as_secs_f64() * 1000.0
                    ))
                    .collect::<Vec<_>>()
                    .join(", ")
            ));
        }
        out
    }

    pub(crate) fn detail(&mut self, name: impl Into<String>, start: Instant) {
        self.details.push((name.into(), start.elapsed()));
    }
    pub(crate) fn phase(&mut self, name: &'static str, start: &mut Instant) {
        self.phases.push((name, start.elapsed()));
        *start = Instant::now();
    }
}

// @ref LLP 3000.007.001#3.11 — builder output names the parent field, never its private target.
pub(super) fn table_label(schema: &crate::Schema, name: &str) -> String {
    if let Some(maintain) = schema.maintain_for(name) {
        return maintain.label();
    }
    for (prefix, suffix) in [("$basis:", "live basis"), ("$recount:", "recount state")] {
        if let Some(target) = name.strip_prefix(prefix) {
            let target = schema
                .maintain_for(target)
                .map_or_else(|| target.to_owned(), |m| m.label());
            return format!("{target} {suffix}");
        }
    }
    name.to_owned()
}

    }

    super::narrowed::transition(tx,next,previous,now,restored_images,&source_definitions)?;

            if !ids.is_empty() {tx.set_meta(&format!("activation_column_restored_ids:{name}.{column}"),&serde_json::to_string(&ids).map_err(|e|RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?)?;}
            tx.conn().execute(
                "DELETE FROM _dormant_values WHERE t=?1 AND column_name=?2 AND NOT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_narrow_rows n WHERE n.t=_dormant_values.t AND n.id=_dormant_values.id)",
                params![t, column],
            )?;
            tx.conn().execute(
                "DELETE FROM _dormant_refs WHERE t=?1 AND column_name=?2 AND NOT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_narrow_rows n WHERE n.t=_dormant_refs.t AND n.id=_dormant_refs.id)",
                params![t, column],
            )?;
            tx.conn().execute(
                "DELETE FROM _dormant_columns WHERE table_name=?1 AND column_name=?2 AND NOT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_values WHERE t=?3 AND column_name=?2)",
                params![name, column,t],
            )?;
        }
        for index in table.indexes.keys() {
            tx.conn().execute(
                "DELETE FROM _dormant_indexes WHERE table_name=?1 AND index_name=?2",
                params![name, index],
            )?;
        }
    }
    Ok(retained_through)
}

fn check_references(tx: &Tx<'_>, next: &Schema) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    for (name, table) in next.tables.iter().filter(|(_, t)| t.ephemeral.is_none()) {
        let t = tx.num(name)?;
        let mut bad = super::adopt_refusals::Samples::default();
        let mut statement=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT DISTINCT d.id,d.column_name,d.parent_id,d.invalid FROM _dormant_refs d LEFT JOIN r p ON p.t=d.parent_t AND p.id=d.parent_id WHERE d.t=?1 AND EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM r child WHERE child.t=d.t AND child.id=d.id) AND (d.invalid=1 OR (p.id IS NULL AND d.invalid!=2)) ORDER BY d.id,d.column_name")?;
        let mut rows = statement.query([t])?;
        while let Some(row) = rows.next()? {
                    let mut edges = Vec::new();
                    if let Ok(value) = row.field(column) {
                        refs(kind, value, &mut edges);
                    }
                    for (parent, parent_id) in edges {
                        let missing = next.table(&parent).is_none()
                            || (tx.get(&parent, &parent_id)?.is_none()
                                && reserved(tx, tx.num(&parent)?, &parent_id)?);
                        if missing {
                            hidden = true;
                        }
                    }
                }
                if !hidden {
                    return Ok(());
                }
                let retained: bool = tx.conn().query_row(
                    "SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_narrow_rows WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2)",
                    params![t, id],
                    |r| r.get(0),
                )?;
                if !retained {
                    newly_retained += 1;
                    let old = source_definitions.get(name).unwrap_or(table);
                    tx.conn().execute(
                        "INSERT INTO _dormant_narrow_rows VALUES(?1,?2,?3,?4,?5)",
                        params![
                            t,
                            id,
                            canonical_json(&row),
                            canonical_json(old),
                            "type or dormant reference"
                        ],
                    )?;
                    for (column, kind) in &old.columns {
                        // Retained cells/whole rows already captured a lifetime; moving them must not erase deletion evidence.
                        let captured:bool=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_refs WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND column_name=?3)",params![t,id,column],|r|r.get(0))?;
                        if captured {
                            continue;
                        }
                        if let Ok(value) = row.field(column) {
                            super::dormant::capture_refs(tx, t, id, column, kind, value)?;
                        }
                        super::dormant::capture_through_parent(tx, name, old, id, column, &row)?;
                        // Nominal keep references may already be dangling. They have no bound lifetime.
                    }
                }
                tx.conn()
                    .execute("DELETE FROM r WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2", params![t, id])?;
                removed += 1;
                Ok(())
            })?;
            tx.clear_activation_scan();
        }
        if removed == 0 {
            break;
        }
    }
    // Consume only rows that survived the closed population, not hidden dependents.
    let restored:u64=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM _dormant_narrow_rows n WHERE EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM r WHERE r.t=n.t AND r.id=n.id)",[],|r|r.get::<_,i64>(0).map(|n|n as u64))?;
    let restored_rows=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT c.name,n.id FROM _dormant_narrow_rows n JOIN _catalog c ON c.num=n.t WHERE EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM r WHERE r.t=n.t AND r.id=n.id)")?.query_map([],|r|Ok((r.get::<_,String>(0)?,r.get::<_,String>(1)?)))?.collect::<Result<Vec<_>,_>>()?;
    let mut restored_tables:std::collections::BTreeMap<String,Vec<String>>=std::collections::BTreeMap::new();
    for (name,id) in restored_rows {restored_tables.entry(name).or_default().push(id);}
    for (name, ids) in restored_tables {
        tx.set_meta(&format!("activation_narrowed_restored:{name}"),&ids.len().to_string())?;
        tx.set_meta(&format!("activation_narrowed_restored_ids:{name}"),&serde_json::to_string(&ids).map_err(|e|RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?)?;
    }
    tx.set_meta("activation_narrowed_retained", &newly_retained.to_string())?;
    tx.set_meta("activation_narrowed_restored", &restored.to_string())?;
    tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _dormant_narrow_rows WHERE EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM r WHERE r.t=_dormant_narrow_rows.t AND r.id=_dormant_narrow_rows.id)",[])?;
    if newly_retained > 0 || restored > 0 {
        tx.set_meta("activation_population", "true")?;
    }
    Ok(())
}

    93	                })))
    94	        });
    95	        let Some((port, web_port)) = matched else { modified.push(path.to_owned()); continue };
    96	        let wanted = super::localize(path, &super::without_design_references(&starter::identity_readme(platform, &title, &wanted_body, personas, &own.offline, own.identity.is_some_and(|(password, guests)| password && !guests))), port, web_port);
    97	        let wanted = if explicit_online { starter::online_readme(&wanted) } else { wanted };

     1	//! One release history for the packaged card and upgrade/check interval.
     2	// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000#11-wave-11-runtime-upgrade-history — no compiler snapshot is required for runtime history.
     3	pub const CHANGELOG: &str = include_str!("../../../packages/snapback4/CHANGELOG.md");
     4	
     5	pub fn version(text: &str) -> Option<(u32, u32, u32)> {
     6	    let mut parts = text.split('.');
     7	    let result = (parts.next()?.parse().ok()?, parts.next()?.parse().ok()?, parts.next()?.split('-').next()?.parse().ok()?);
     8	    parts.next().is_none().then_some(result)
     9	}
    10	pub fn between(history: &str, previous: Option<&str>, installed: &str) -> String {
    11	    let Some(installed) = version(installed) else { return String::new(); };
    12	    let previous = previous.and_then(version);
    13	    let mut selected = false;
    14	    let mut out = String::new();
    15	    for line in history.lines() {
    16	        if let Some(release) = line.strip_prefix("## ") {
    17	            selected = version(release).is_some_and(|v| v <= installed && previous.is_none_or(|old| v > old));
    18	        }
    19	        if selected && line.starts_with("## ") { if !out.is_empty() { out.push('\n'); } out.push_str(line); out.push_str("\n\n"); }
    20	        else if selected && line.starts_with("- ") { out.push_str(line); out.push('\n'); }
    21	    }
    22	    out
    23	}
    24	pub fn interval(previous: &str, installed: &str) -> String {
    25	    if version(previous).zip(version(installed)).is_none_or(|(old, new)| old >= new) { return String::new(); }
    26	    let entries = between(CHANGELOG, Some(previous), installed);
    27	    if entries.is_empty() { return String::new(); }
    28	    format!("runtime changes: {previous} → {installed}\n{entries}History begins at 0.4.26; earlier changes are not recorded.\n")
    29	}
    54	}
    55	export function refuseCredentials(env = process.env) {
    56	  for (const key of ["NPM_TOKEN", "NODE_AUTH_TOKEN"]) if (Object.hasOwn(env, key))
    57	    throw new Error(`Linux qualification refuses ${key}; unset it before qualification (no authenticated registry writes)`);
    58	}
    59	function validAttempts(proofs, starter) {
    60	  return Array.isArray(proofs) && proofs.length === 2 && [1, 2].every(attempt => proofs.some(p =>
    61	    p.starter === starter.id && p.kind === starter.kind && p.attempt === attempt && p.status === "passed" && Array.isArray(p.range) && p.range.length === 2
    62	    && Number.isInteger(p.range[0]) && Number.isInteger(p.range[1]) && p.range[0] > 0 && p.range[1] <= 65535 && p.range[0] < p.range[1]
    63	    && p.occupied === p.range[0] && p.cpuLoadWorkers >= 1));
    64	}
    65	export function validateFrontendProofs(proofs) {
    66	  if (!Array.isArray(proofs) || proofs.length !== starterCases.length * 2 || !starterCases.every(s => validAttempts(proofs.filter(p => p.starter === s.id), s)))
    67	    throw new Error("Linux receipt needs every generated frontend/template proof twice under occupied-port and CPU load");
    68	}
    69	export function validateStarterProofs(starters) {
    70	  if (!Array.isArray(starters) || starters.length !== starterCases.length) throw Error("Linux receipt needs every starter toolchain and frontend proof");
    71	  validateFrontendProofs(starters.flatMap(s => s.frontendProofs ?? []));
    72	  for (const starter of starterCases) {
    73	    const result = starters.find(s => s.starter === starter.id);
    74	    if (!result || result.status !== "passed" || result.typecheck !== "passed" || result.build !== "passed"
    75	        || (["web", "expo"].includes(starter.id) && !validAttempts(result.adaptedProofs, starter)))
    76	      throw Error("Linux receipt needs every starter toolchain and frontend proof, including adapted Home");
    77	  }
    78	}
    79	export function validateLinux(receipt, tarball, client, source) {
    80	  if (receipt.format !== 1 || receipt.platform?.name !== platforms[1] || receipt.platform.version !== client.version || receipt.source !== source || !/^[a-f0-9]{40}$/.test(source ?? ""))
    81	    throw new Error("Linux receipt source/version/platform does not match the prepared release");
    82	  if (receipt.target !== linuxTarget || receipt.flags?.LIBSQLITE3_FLAGS !== sqliteBuildFlags || receipt.flags?.RUSTFLAGS !== linuxRustFlags || !receipt.toolchain?.rustc || !receipt.toolchain?.cc || !receipt.toolchain?.linker)
    83	    throw new Error("Linux receipt target/toolchain/SQLite flags are incomplete or differ");

     1	//! Recover a version, not invented compiler facts, from an older app.
     2	use super::*;
     3	use std::fs;
     4	// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000#11-wave-11-runtime-upgrade-history — history acknowledgment is independent of compiler qualification.
     5	
     6	fn exact(text: &str) -> Option<String> {
     7	    snapback4_lang::release_changes::version(text).map(|_| text.to_owned())
     8	}
     9	fn json_version(text: &str, key: &str) -> Option<String> {
    10	    let value: Json = serde_json::from_str(text).ok()?;
    11	    exact(value[key].as_str()?)
    12	}
    13	pub(super) fn locked_version(text: &str) -> Option<String> {
    14	    if let Ok(lock) = serde_json::from_str::<Json>(text) {
    15	        if let Some(version) = lock.pointer("/packages/node_modules~1snapback4/version").or_else(|| lock.pointer("/dependencies/snapback4/version")).and_then(Json::as_str).and_then(exact) { return Some(version); }
    16	    }
    17	    // Bun's JSONC package tuple; pnpm's importer resolution (not specifier).
    18	    let mut versions = std::collections::BTreeSet::new();
    19	    for (position, _) in text.match_indices("snapback4@") {
    20	        let tail = &text[position + "snapback4@".len()..];
    21	        let candidate = tail.split(|c: char| !(c.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || matches!(c, '.' | '-'))).next().unwrap_or("");
    22	        if let Some(version) = exact(candidate) { versions.insert(version); }
    23	    }
    24	    let lines: Vec<_> = text.lines().collect();
    25	    for (index, line) in lines.iter().enumerate() {
    26	        if line.trim().trim_matches('\'').trim_matches('"') == "snapback4:" {
    27	            for line in lines.iter().skip(index + 1).take(4) {
    28	                if let Some(value) = line.trim().strip_prefix("version:") {
    29	                    let value = value.trim().trim_matches(['\'', '"']).split('(').next().unwrap_or("");
    30	                    if let Some(version) = exact(value) { versions.insert(version); }
    31	                }
    32	            }
    33	        }
    34	    }
    35	    (versions.len() == 1).then(|| versions.into_iter().next()).flatten()
    36	}
    37	pub(super) fn previous(project: &Project) -> Option<String> {
    38	    let read_version = |path, key| fs::read_to_string(path).ok().and_then(|text| json_version(&text, key));
    39	    if let Some(version) = read_version(project.data_dir().join("runtime-version.json"), "version") { return Some(version); }
    40	    if let Some(version) = read_version(project.data_dir().join("check-surface.json"), "version") { return Some(version); }
    41	    if let Some(version) = read_version(project.data_dir().join("upgrade-version.json"), "version") { return Some(version); }
    42	    if let Some(version) = read_version(project.generated_dir().join("package.json"), "snapback4Version") { return Some(version); }
    43	    if let Some(version) = read_version(project.generated_dir().join("package.json"), "version") { return Some(version); }
    44	    for name in ["package-lock.json", "npm-shrinkwrap.json", "bun.lock", "pnpm-lock.yaml"] {
    45	        if let Some(version) = fs::read_to_string(project.root.join(name)).ok().and_then(|text| locked_version(&text)) { return Some(version); }
    46	    }
    47	    None
    48	}
    49	pub(super) fn report(project: &Project, json: bool) {

    53	        if !text.is_empty() { if json { eprint!("{text}"); } else { print!("{text}"); } }
    54	    }
    55	    let save = || -> std::io::Result<()> {
    56	        fs::create_dir_all(project.data_dir())?;

    60	        fs::rename(temporary, path)

/// Wave 7 (R3, 5 lanes: 004 038 064 082 091): beside an all-`online only`
/// schema the README said "It works offline and syncs when the link returns"
/// and init's summary "offline-capable". Both now follow the synced tables, at
/// init and at `--adapt`, and adapt still recognizes 0.4.22's README.
#[test]
fn the_readme_says_what_works_offline_from_the_schemas_synced_tables() {
    let online = "use identity\ntable orders:\n  owner: principal\n  title: text 1..80\n  read <- .owner = viewer\n  insert <- .owner = viewer\n  online only\nquery mine(c: cursor ?):\n  return orders where .owner = viewer first 20 by .id after c\n";
    let synced = format!("{online}table notes:\n  owner: principal\n  body: text <=200\n  read <- .owner = viewer\n  insert <- .owner = viewer\n  sync to .owner\n");
    for flag in ["--web", "--expo"] {
        let dir = Dir::new();
        fs::create_dir_all(dir.0.join("snapback")).unwrap();
        fs::write(dir.0.join("snapback/schema.q"), online).unwrap();
        let output = dir.init(&[flag]);
        let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout);
        assert!(output.status.success() && stdout.contains("online reads (no table declares `sync`") && !stdout.contains("offline-capable"), "{flag}: {stdout}");
        let readme = fs::read_to_string(dir.0.join("README.md")).unwrap();
        let flat = readme.replace('\n', " ");
        assert!(!readme.contains("works offline") && !readme.contains("work offline"), "{flag}: {readme}");
        assert!(flat.contains("no table in `snapback/*.q` declares `sync`, so reads need the server. Writes made offline are kept") && flat.contains(if flag == "--web" { "principal journal" } else { "on this device" }), "{flag}: {readme}");
        assert!(readme.lines().all(|line| line.matches('`').count() % 2 == 0 || line.starts_with("```") || line.contains("``")), "{flag}: no code span is wrapped: {readme}");
        // The schema grows a synced table: adapt rewrites only that paragraph.
        fs::write(dir.0.join("snapback/schema.q"), &synced).unwrap();
        let checked = Command::new(BIN).arg("check").current_dir(&dir.0).output().unwrap();
        assert!(String::from_utf8_lossy(&checked.stdout).contains("README.md: the unmodified starter README says what works offline for another schema"), "{flag}: {checked:?}");
        let adapted = dir.init(&["--adapt"]);
        assert!(adapted.status.success() && String::from_utf8_lossy(&adapted.stdout).contains("rewrote") && String::from_utf8_lossy(&adapted.stdout).contains("README.md"), "{flag}: {adapted:?}");
        let after = fs::read_to_string(dir.0.join("README.md")).unwrap();
        if flag == "--web" { assert!(fs::read_to_string(dir.0.join("src/client.ts")).unwrap().contains("device: webDevice()"), "adapt follows the newly synced table"); }
        assert!(after.replace('\n', " ").contains("the synced table (`notes`) reads and writes offline"), "{flag}: {after}");
        assert_eq!(after.split("\n\n").filter(|paragraph| !paragraph.starts_with("Offline")).collect::<Vec<_>>(),
            readme.replace("web uses the principal write journal (no synced tables).", "web uses the wasm\ndevice.").replace("or the principal write journal (web, no synced tables).", "or `webDevice()` (web).").split("\n\n").filter(|paragraph| !paragraph.starts_with("Offline")).collect::<Vec<_>>(), "{flag}: only offline wording changes");
        assert!(dir.init(&["--adapt"]).status.success());
        assert_eq!(fs::read_to_string(dir.0.join("README.md")).unwrap(), after, "{flag}: adapt is idempotent");
        // 0.4.22's README (the unconditional claim) is still the starter's, and is rewritten.
        let legacy = match flag {
            "--web" => after.split("\n\n").filter(|paragraph| !paragraph.starts_with("Offline")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join("\n\n")
                .replace("the Snapback 4 web starter.", "the Snapback 4 web starter. It works offline and syncs when the link returns."),
            _ => after.split("\n\n").filter(|paragraph| !paragraph.starts_with("Offline")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join("\n\n")
                .replace("open the native device; Expo Go uses the online client; web uses the wasm\ndevice.", "open the native device and work offline; Expo Go uses the online client; web\nuses the wasm device."),
        };
        assert_ne!(legacy, after);
        fs::write(dir.0.join("README.md"), &legacy).unwrap();
        assert!(dir.init(&["--adapt"]).status.success());
        assert_eq!(fs::read_to_string(dir.0.join("README.md")).unwrap(), after, "{flag}: 0.4.22's README is rewritten");
        // A README the project edited is never touched.
        fs::write(dir.0.join("README.md"), format!("{after}\nMy notes.\n")).unwrap();
        fs::write(dir.0.join("snapback/schema.q"), online).unwrap();
        assert!(dir.init(&["--adapt"]).status.success());
        assert!(fs::read_to_string(dir.0.join("README.md")).unwrap().ends_with("My notes.\n"));
    }
# What Snapback 4 Does Not Do

Longer than the doing-list on purpose. Moving something off it means writing one
line naming what it unblocks and taking something off the doing-list in the same
change. Nothing comes off during slice one (LLP 3000 §3: the clock is four weeks).

## The bars

The bakeoff gates in [LLP 3000 §3](../llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md) are the
bars: the loop bar, hub acquisition within 2× the measured floor, client bytes
under the competitor-anchored budgets, the six scenarios green in the simulator
on the hub fixture. Everything not required to pass them does not exist yet.

## Not building

- **A second authoring language.** Quarry, one spelling, copied from Snapback 2 (LLP 1004, LLP 1007 §4b). TypeScript is for seeds, journeys, effect bodies and the client.
- **Byte- and step-priced certification.** No closure, heap, output or step ceilings at `check`; the certificate certifies shape, uniformity, completeness class and prediction eligibility. Resources are metered at runtime (LLP 2000.000 §6).
- **A per-query result cache.** No range receipts, validators, halos, absence evidence, coverage spans, extent facts, held directories, fact integrity or swap journals. The client replicates declared partitions with one watermark each (LLP 2000.000 §7).
- **Engine-minted ids for client inserts, grants, ID slices.** Ids are client-minted, time-ordered, 128-bit; the engine mints for its own writes.
- **The `drain` protocol, follower-tab inbox rows, `defineItems`, `withHistory`, `mutationTarget`, `receipt.source`, `local.caughtUp`, `partial` reason codes.** The card (`packages/snapback4/src/contract.ts`) is the whole client vocabulary; `why` carries the rest.
- **`retain` as a receipt rule.** `until-revoked` and `delivered-history` survive as the client's leave policy on a scope tombstone.
- **JavaScript in the data plane.** Queries and mutations run in the Rust interpreter inside the SQLite transaction, and in the same interpreter on the client (host decided at week 3 by measurement, LLP 2000.000 §9). A *job* may be the app's own Rust, hosted as wasm through the interpreter's operations under the job's meter (LLP 3000.005, the Moves line below); a query or mutation may not.
- **An embedded JS engine for effects.** The data plane never calls out. `emit` writes the effect durably in the mutation's commit and an external worker holding a service token claims, performs and settles it over the wire (`snapback4/worker`); the four reference effects run on the builder's Node (LLP 2000.000 §10, decided 2026-09-13).
- **Committed generated code; an auth mode; a supervised bundler.** As Snapback 2 (`rules/NOT-DOING.md`): types by inference, personas not modes, `SNAPBACK_URL` is the client contract.
- **A TypeScript twin of the interpreter.** Retired by the author's ruling of 2026-09-13 (LLP 3000 §7): one implementation, in Rust, on every host — the core's SQLite under the native module, memory under wasm on the web with the host persisting each commit's changes. The web pays 312,924 B gzip before the diet where the twin's tier paid 16,842; the conformance obligation between two interpreters ends. Expo Go runs the online tier.
- **More than one hosted target, an admin console, media transcoding or thumbnails, push delivery itself (a worker's), cron syntax beyond `every N at HH:MM`, vector or relevance-ranked search (word-prefix search is in: `words(..)` and `matching`), federation, multi-region, device-to-device sync, at-rest encryption claims.** After the clock, on evidence.
- **General observability beyond `why`, `trace`, `logs`, `data`, `sql`, server operation latency, and bounded client subscription inspection.** The `sql` verb is read-only inspection of the developer's own store (LLP 3000.007 §8.8), never an application surface. Separately declared application SQL reads are admitted for increment D (LLP 3000.009 §6.2, §14.4; the 2026-09-16 Moves line below), not implemented by this inspection verb.
- **A guide corpus.** The compiler's facts, one language card and keyword cards, as Snapback 2; no freshness dates. Every card's Quarry is compiled by the suite and the language card is run, so a card cannot promise a form the parser refuses.

- **Partial union delivery, two group audiences, a public branch.** A union syncs principal-column branches and at most one group branch, each a whole disjunct of the read rule (LLP 3000.036.000.000); a branch with an extra condition, a second group or a `public` branch is refused with the rewrite, never partially delivered; service grants add no device audience (LLP 3000.007 §20.6). A union does not combine with delivered history, read-through children, synced or row-keyed views, owner keys, timelines, private field owners, acquisition windows or direct/inherited expiry on content or membership scopes.
- **Exact horizon maintenance.** The horizon bounds acquisition (a snapshot, a bootstrap); the log never evicts or back-fills to hold a device's group at exactly N rows. A local read past the acquired horizon by key says `complete: false`.

- **Clock expressions in rules.** A read rule compares with `=` and `!=` only; `.expiresAt > now` remains a mutation `require`. Stored lifetimes use `not before .col` for server-controlled opening and `expire at .col` for expiry. The engine publishes opening commits; devices receive no unopened payload. The supported compositions and explicit exclusions are in LLP 3000.036.003.
- **A jobs framework.** No priorities, worker pools or dead letters: one FIFO of runs, one chunk at a time, a refused chunk parked with its refusal. Actions, effects and scheduled actions carry per-operation retries, deadlines and cancellation, and a record per external step (LLP 3000.009 §5.2; the Moves line below). Missed period boundaries while a server is down fire once on return, not once per missed period.
- **Asset processing.** No transcoding, thumbnails, rungs or HEIC: the bytes are served as uploaded; JPEG, PNG, WebP and MP4 only; image dimensions and optional MP4 duration read from the header; MP4 dimensions are not extracted.
- **Search beyond word prefixes.** One tokenizer (NFC, lowercase, alphanumeric words), one index shape (`scope, words(cols), order.., id`), one read (`matching`): every term a prefix of some word, the first term's words merged as lanes, bounded by the meter in a uniform scope, at most 32 words in an admitted non-uniform scope. No stemming, no ranking by relevance, no phrase or mid-word matching, no per-language folding, no intersection index for the second term (it filters the page).
- **An identity provider.** Guests, password accounts, service seats, and sessions a service mints for accounts the application authenticated itself. No phone, OAuth, magic links or MFA in the engine: an application's worker verifies those and mints the session.
- **No multi-server presence, no presence history or last-seen storage, no broadcast event stream, no connection-bound rows.** `connected[p]` stays server-wide, counting held polls and their 35 s grace; a live query that may read it re-runs in the background about every 5 seconds to stay current (LLP 3000.018). Typing and viewing use short-lived, authorized ephemeral tables in one server process (LLP 3000.017); they never sync or decide durable invariants. A device does not know presence and says so.

## Process not doing

- **2026-09-28 — ordinary mixed search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3, lane OS round 1).
  Admit the SDK's bounded merge of current server rows and certified Left images
  for the proved single time-ordered word page. This removes application-owned
  online/held selection for that shape. One server call per requested page;
  recomputation pays again from consumed positions. The coordinator shares the
  existing device read allowance. No durable search session, extra synced rows,
  new authority or additional allowance is admitted.

- **2026-09-28 — inferred current-scope word search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3,
  lane OS). A single bare ordinary word page may derive the exact non-expiring
  membership audience for the existing compact server plan. This removes the
  requirement to repeat that audience predicate in this narrow server query.
  The compiler supplies a scoped words index when absent; it is an ordinary
  schema index, maintained on writes and on devices, with no extra synced rows
  or new authority. The device keeps its original bare scan. Round 0 left ordered composition
  and combined accounting outside its independent subset; round 1 above admits
  that bounded SDK composition. Durable search sessions remain excluded.
- **2026-10-12 — union sync audiences** (LLP 3000.036.000.000, workstream UNION, two blind review rounds). A table syncs to the union of principal-column audiences and one group audience; a device holds a row while any branch admits its viewer. What it unblocks: owner-or-team, buyer-or-seller and owner-or-staff tables offline for every reader, the shape three app farms asked for. Taken off: "one `sync` audience per table". Partial delivery, two groups and a public branch stay excluded (above).
- **2026-09-28 — explicit owner-key coverage** (LLP 3000.028 §3.4, Lane OK commission). A public window may opt into a compiler-owned, horizon-free principal key partition to answer ordinary own-key existence offline. Taken off: the exclusion of narrower platform key distribution beside one public window. General union audiences, full own-row replication and per-query absence caches remain excluded. The projection uses existing metered maintains, adoption and whole-partition publication; existing windows do not expand.
- **2026-09-26 — a live query keeps presence current** (LLP 3000.018). A 0.4.2 probe found an observed `connected[p]` reporting `fresh: true` while stale for over a minute, and a comparative builder polled around it. What it unblocks: online marks and presence-aware lists that stay current. Taken off: "a presence stream" as an exclusion for live queries; a presence API, last-seen storage and multi-server presence stay out. Under the author's standing authorization for reworks that make queries excellent (2026-09-25).

- **No drift registries, inventories, line-pinned diagnostics registries, claim digests or frozen-record gates.** Generated projections are built, not committed.
- **No gate bound to an exact commit; no push gated on a battery.** Five blocking checks in sixty seconds, run before push; everything slower on a schedule with bounded tickets.
- **No ceiling raised in slice one.** Budgets are anchored to a competitor's measured number and recorded once in the journal.
- **No standing panel** except the two blind reviews LLP 3000 decision 4 names (partition inversion; the meter's cut on non-uniform scans). The author explicitly commissioned the one-off LLP 3000.008 panel and disjoint Astra implementation lanes on 2026-09-14; its bounded disposition is recorded in that umbrella and LLP 3000 §7.
- **No new LLP number** without first extending LLP 3000 or LLP 2000.000; no spec without running code (`rules/RULES.md`: only implemented specs bind).
- **No second session on the core** during slice one; lanes take the periphery.
- **No test matrix as the primary harness.** The simulator is; real-browser and device witnesses cover only what it cannot reach.
- **No files over 1,500 lines**, including ones copied from Snapback 2. Split on
  the way in. The one exception is the package's published documents
  (`packages/snapback4/README.md` and its linked references): the rule exists to
  keep a *source* file reviewable, and the 0.2.25 calibration measured the cost of
  applying it to a document — splitting the README was that run's single largest
  scoring loss, because the builder then had four places to look instead of one
  (LLP 3000.007 §20.19). A published document is bounded by whether a builder can
  find one way in, not by a line count; the census that enforces this rule covers
  source, and says so.

## Deliberately worse

- No compatibility with Snapback 1 or Snapback 2 apps, packages or on-disk formats.
- No API stability before the clock; every publish is `0.0.x`.
- Sparse prose documentation; the diagnostics and the recipes carry the load.
- One hosted target, SQLite in the production CLI, one platform binary at a time. A separately enabled Postgres evaluation adapter is not a hosted/deployable target (Moves below).

## Moves

- **2026-10-13 — stored lower lifetimes (`not before`) are commissioned by PAGE2** (LLP 3000.036.003). This unblocks timed disclosure without an application publication job. Per-application jobs that flip a publication flag leave this admitted path; clock expressions in rules, captured lower lifetimes for read-through children, and boundary recounts for maintained data remain excluded. HS owns handshake transport; PAGE2 declares the new semantic capability.

- **2026-10-06 — exact Following history (FH delegated round-1 ruling).**
  Admit a Following-specific server traversal and bounded volatile frontier/replay
  state under LLP 3000.026 §9. The approved design reads exact history online
  from the first page; the SDK will own continuation. The first milestone is internal event metadata
  and the oracle, with no public completeness claim. Taken off: a blanket ban on
  state for this one expiring read. General result caches, resumable queries,
  device traversal, inbox coverage certificates and history-only blocks remain
  excluded. Existing inbox bytes and runtime ceilings remain unchanged.

- **2026-09-26 — server operation latency (LLP 3000.019 §3.7).** Calibration #22
  measured each read once and could not claim repeated p95/degradation testing.
  Unblocks percentile measurements of the application's own load without a
  custom timing harness. Taken off: a latency harness written by the builder;
  general metrics, tracing infrastructure and an admin console remain excluded.

- **2026-09-23 — Quarry states intent moves onto the doing-list** (Charlie:
  "if you need to try making *significant* architectural reworks in order to
  make the data modeling, queries, and scalability be excellent and elegant,
  you can experiment with that"; LLP 3000.013). Unblocks reads that state
  predicates and order, one `view` declaration for derived data, Following
  without caps, jobs and aggregates without author-chosen bounds, an app
  object and an offline web shell. Taken off: index names and author-chosen
  engine numbers on the paved path; the timeline's promotion, pull routes and
  caps; the "Time in rules" refusal is unchanged. Stands: one interpreter, no
  JavaScript in the data plane, no lowering Quarry to SQL, no relevance-ranked
  search.

- **2026-09-16 — the remaining general-purpose program moves onto the doing-list**
  (Charlie: "complete everything ... we'll remove services when we are done
  with the workroom test rather than wait to add them in ... use the fleet";
  LLP 3000.009 §14.7). Unblocks the §7 integrated-service baseline, C3 production
  Postgres, D's authorized database capabilities and measurement-led C2/E
  work while Workroom continues. Taken off: waiting for Workroom before
  starting services, and keeping production Postgres integration outside the
  current implementation program. The completed Workroom record drives a

I also found a history gap for accepted prerelease versions: JavaScript selects the stable release entry, but Rust drops or rejects the prerelease suffix and can acknowledge the stable version without showing that entry. The new prerelease test checks only JavaScript ordering.

Qualification is still pending. I’ll list that separately from these code findings.

codex
Reviewed `81cb4124aa..4e96153fdf`, including qualifier precursor `5915c7e361`. Two material issues remain:

- **P2 — Older generated READMEs stop adapting.** [starter.rs:384](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w12-fix28/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/verbs/init/starter.rs:384) now appends proof instructions to every recognition candidate, while [adapt.rs:90](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w12-fix28/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/verbs/init/adapt.rs:90) still requires matching those bytes. A read-only dry run against retained `target/fix28-before-starters-2/starter-web` labels its untouched README as application-owned. Subsequent adaptation preserves stale screen, identity and offline instructions; it can also unexpectedly become preview-only. Preserve recognition of pre-appendix bytes and test an actual historical README.

- **P2 — Prerelease history can be skipped permanently.** [release_changes.rs:5](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w12-fix28/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/release_changes.rs:5) discards `-rc` and rejects dotted suffixes such as `-rc.1`. With acknowledgment `0.4.28-rc` and installed `0.4.28`, Rust treats the versions as equal, prints no history, then [upgrade_history.rs:59](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w12-fix28/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/verbs/upgrade_history.rs:59) acknowledges stable `0.4.28`. JavaScript selects the `0.4.28` entry correctly. This violates the exact interval contract for versions the upgrade CLI accepts. Align Rust/JavaScript parsing and ordering; test history through real `check`, rather than only JavaScript comparison.

Existing logs show 137 client and 47 dormant tests passing; root reference, inventory and guide checks pass. All recorded before-log hashes match.

Separate completion follow-up: finish and retain the fresh packed Linux after evidence—12 PROOF attempts, EXPOFIX’s separate 12 plus four adapted attempts, and Ubuntu/Alpine builds—alongside remaining check results. I have not treated pending evidence as a pass.

No files edited, Cargo/full suite run, commits, pushes, or other-worktree access.

**NOT READY**
